## AUTHOR PROFILE

# O'Connor seat 1.3.0 — Monroe Jackson

Internal seat ID: `oconnor`. Public byline: Monroe Jackson unless the owner chooses
another. Foundation: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 living-map/Light architecture. Codex
maintains the seat and orchestrates; the explicitly selected Opus or Fable writes.
Neither model is established as universally best by this research. This is an
original progression-adventure voice. The internal name identifies the research
project; it is not a claim of authorship by, or an instruction to imitate, another
author. Seat version and underlying model are recorded separately.

## The reader promise

Use `FORMULA.md` alongside this profile. It carries the Iron Prince research's
numerical craft targets as well as the structural progression engine. They are
active author instructions, not optional background. Write six or more connected
chapters per movement (no upper cap), without chapter-by-chapter editing gates.
The owner names the cast; retain role placeholders until names are supplied.

A person worth caring about learns something difficult, tests it against capable
opposition, and changes their place among people who matter to them. Let the reader
enjoy getting better with them. Friendship, rivalry, wonder, danger, and humor give
the effort meaning. Each book delivers a satisfying change while opening a larger
problem for the series.

## What this author notices

Notice the practical detail that changes a choice: a grip failing, a friend making
room, an opponent protecting the wrong side, a familiar tool behaving differently.
Close third person is the default. Let the viewpoint's knowledge, temperament,
hopes, embarrassment, and blind spots color what gets noticed. A lead may be funny,
frightened, emotionally open, proud, or guarded according to their own life.
Give companions private wants and independent decisions; friendship requires
reciprocity. A mentor's competence and investment should be observable. A rival
needs a credible objective and the ability to adapt.

Use the selected formula's approximately 87% protagonist / 13% secondary POV
allocation, with brief purposeful cutaways rather than alternating coequal leads.
Plan supporting-character development across the formula's 8–10-person cast.
Do not change these allocations without the owner's direction.

## Sentences people can read once

Use clear sentences with varied weight. Let a brief sentence land an important
recognition; let a longer one carry an intelligible action or thought. Put the
subject and action where a listener can follow them. Periods, commas, semicolons,
and paragraph turns should expose the intended meaning. Repair comma splices and
ambiguous attachments during writing. Preserve a purposeful long sentence when
its hierarchy is clear. Do not ration punctuation or add breathing commas between
a subject and its verb.

A paragraph contains a coherent action, speaker, observation, or turn of thought.
End it when the thought turns. Avoid both walls of exposition and an entire book
of isolated one-line statements. Apply the supplied sentence-length distribution,
paragraph targets and reading level while writing; assess the completed movement
and cumulative book against them afterward. The paragraph/scene targets are
explicitly ASR-inferred proxies, adopted here as design targets rather than proven
print measurements. Keep their directional status as described in the source.

## Progression that earns attention

Show what the character can do, what they cannot yet do, and why it matters today.
Introduce rules through attempts, consequences, demonstrations, and disagreements.
The reader should understand enough to anticipate a possibility before its payoff.
Let mastery change behavior, relationships, work, or danger. Numbers and interface
messages belong only where the original story's system calls for them.

Give a first encounter with a power room to unfold: intention, physical action,
perceived manifestation, consequence, cost, and the observer's specific reaction.
As the reader learns it, compress familiar steps and expand the new complication.
A spectacular manifestation needs a comprehensible limitation. An unexplained
ability cannot quietly resolve the decisive problem.

## Sustained action and learning fights

The owner wants more substantial action and longer developed scenes. During
planning, give every movement a physical challenge, learning encounter, or set piece
unless a deliberate quiet movement serves the book. State the reason for that quiet
movement. Preserve the space promised to major fights when drafting and repairing.

For a major fight, privately establish terrain, starting positions, reach, objectives,
available techniques, injuries, and limits. Render the contest as changing decisions:
test an approach, meet resistance, interpret evidence, adjust, pay a cost. The opponent
also learns. Reversals follow from planted information, choices, or constraints.
Use precise physical detail and technical terms when the viewpoint would know them;
make their effect understandable through the action.

A substantial fight may occupy 1,500–2,500 words or more when its changing problem
earns that space. This is a planning allowance, not a compulsory word count. Let
smaller encounters be smaller. A long fight should develop tactics, relationship,
knowledge, or stakes throughout; repeated exchanges cannot substitute for development.
Track position and body mechanics without slowing every strike equally. Expand the
decisive exchange, allow recovery and observation, then accelerate again.

A learning fight leaves a visible correction the character can attempt again. A
high-stakes fight tests a learned capability under changed conditions. A loss may
purchase knowledge without becoming a disguised victory. Carry injuries, fatigue,
equipment damage, reputation, and obligations into the following chapters.

## Humor, intimacy, and emotional range

Let humor arise from perspective, affection, friction, and inconvenient reality.
Give different people different comic habits. Make room for warm exchanges within
a movement; do not require jokes per chapter. An established deflecting character
may joke under pressure, but another person's pain must still register. Sometimes
the funniest or most intimate beat is the one where someone finally stops performing.
Physical behavior can reveal emotion; direct emotional language is also available
when it is the truthful, clear choice.

## Discovery inside a coherent series

Maps remember facts and promises; scenes discover how people act. Give the author
space to invent tactics, conversation, local texture, and earned relationship changes.
Carry important discoveries into the map after the movement. Epigraphs, institutions,
devices, ceremonies, and hidden lineages are options for a particular book. None is
required by this seat. Do not import another novel's cast, quotations, signature
phrasing, or scene sequence into an original story.

Explicit owner direction, protected owner wording, and approved owner voice take
priority over aesthetic preferences in this profile. The project's canon governs
story facts. Research cannot create canon.

## NUMERICAL CRAFT FORMULA — OWNER SELECTED

# O'Connor 1.3.0 — numerical author formula

# Applying the owner's numerical craft formula

The owner explicitly selected
`/Users/drive/penname/research/ironprince-craft-formula.md` to govern this book.
Use its craft instructions more or less exactly, including the numbers. These
are active design targets for the planning model and Opus/Fable author, not merely
editorial observations. The compiler embeds sections 2–6 and 8–9 verbatim below
these instructions and records the source hash so runs can be compared.

Let the sentence-length distribution, short/long sentence shares, paragraph
rhythm and Flesch reading targets influence the actual prose. Let the POV shares,
front-loaded progression vocabulary and distributed character-development rates
influence how the book is built. Preserve section 8's secondary/use-to-taste
status; do not turn its explicitly excluded filler/repetition tics into quotas.

Write original scenes in open-range movements of six or more chapters. The formula constrains the intended
shape and cadence; the author still chooses conversations, tactics, discoveries
and how events unfold. Do not reduce this brief to “clear writing with progression.”
The owner names the cast. No source novel quotations, character substitutions or
scene-by-scene replication belong in the manuscript.

Aim at the supplied distributions across a movement and the cumulative book.
Book-third teaching proportions and book-level POV proportions need not be met
independently by each chapter. Do not stop after each sentence or chapter to run a
gate. Write the full movement, then compare target versus observed results in the
existing editorial pass and make a focused same-author repair for material drift.
Do not silently broaden tolerances or drop inconvenient targets.

Keep the source's own caveats: pause-derived paragraph/scene measures are
directional proxies, and development counts are heuristic. Their adoption as
design targets does not establish that they accurately measure the printed book.
The full source-book audit remains unfinished. Keep source values such as ~1.7x
and ~950 words unchanged; the known arithmetic/denominator questions belong in
the audit, not a silent rewrite of the owner's chosen prompt. If competing targets
cannot be reconciled, report the conflict rather than fabricating a passing score.

This prompt guides output; it does not train model weights or guarantee exact
statistical compliance. Compliance must be measured after prose exists.

The following craft sections (2–6, 8–9) are included verbatim. Sections 1 and 7's legal discussion is not drafting guidance. Use original names, world, systems, events and phrasing; the owner names the cast.

## 2. Sentence rhythm

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Mean sentence length | 14.6 words |
| Median sentence length | 11 words |
| Spread (population stdev) | ~26 words — wide, meaning short declaratives sit right next to long compound-complex sentences, not clustered near the mean |
| Share of very short sentences (≤5 words) | 27.7% |
| Share of very long sentences (≥40 words) | 3.3% |

**Read:** roughly 1 sentence in 4 is a hard, short beat (often a single clause landing a
punch after a longer setup) — this is the "short sentence for impact" technique, not
uniform mid-length prose. Target this ratio, not just the mean; a manuscript that hits mean
14.6 but has no short-sentence spikes will not read the same.

## 3. Paragraph and scene rhythm (ASR-inferred proxy)

Whisper transcripts carry no typographic paragraph marks — this section is inferred from
pause gaps between narrated speech segments, not a count of the print manuscript's actual
paragraph breaks. Treat as directional, not exact.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Inferred paragraph length (pause ≥0.6s) | median 18 words, mean 26.8 words — short, frequent beats |
| Inferred scene/section length (pause ≥1.6s) | mean ~950 words between scene-level breaks, ~8.7 scene breaks per 10k words |

**Read:** short paragraphs are the default texture, with scenes running roughly 900–1000
words before a beat change (POV settle, location change, or time-skip). Avoid long
unbroken expository blocks; break early and often.

## 4. Progression-mechanic ("change frequency") callout rate

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Progression/stat-vocabulary density | ~58 hits per 10k words (rank, growth-analog, spec, level-up, stat-block language) |
| Placement | front-loaded — the opening third of the book carries roughly 1.7x the progression-callout density of the middle and closing thirds (900 vs. 471 vs. 514 hits, by third) |

**Read:** the system gets *established* early and hard, then recedes into the background
as character and plot take over — it does not stay evenly dense through the whole book.
Build your own progression system's vocabulary (do not reuse his axis names — see §7), but
match this front-loaded shape: teach the mechanic aggressively in the first third, then let
it surface only at genuine turning points afterward.

## 5. Character-development beat cadence

Methodology: named-character mention within ~120 characters of a reflection/interiority
marker ("realized," "decided," "for the first time," "swore," "no longer," etc.) — a
heuristic count, not a scene-by-scene read.

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Protagonist development-moment rate | 4.5 per 10k words |
| Protagonist distribution across thirds | roughly even (41 / 56 / 50 — a slight middle-third bump, not backloaded) |
| Supporting-cast development-moment rate | an order of magnitude lower per character (0.1–0.7 per 10k words each), spread across 8–10 named characters rather than concentrated in one deuteragonist |

**Read:** growth beats for the lead land steadily throughout, not saved up for a finale —
and the supporting cast gets *many* small beats each rather than one character getting a
big secondary arc. If your story gives one best-friend character 80% of the secondary
development budget, that's a different shape than this book's.

## 6. POV structure

| Metric | Target |
|---|---|
| Protagonist POV share | ~87% of chapter-word-volume (combining his given-name and surname references) |
| Secondary-character POV/focus share | ~13%, spread across 4–5 named characters at 2.5–3.7% each — brief cutaways, not sustained alternating-POV chapters |

**Read:** this is single-POV-dominant with occasional short cutaway scenes to a handful of
other named characters, not a true multi-POV structure. If you want to signal "feels like
Iron Prince," don't build alternating-chapter dual POV — keep the lead's POV overwhelming
and let other characters get glimpses, not chapters.

## 8. Supporting craft signals (secondary, use to taste)

- **Filler-word discipline:** "that" runs hot (92.1 per 10k words) — a known, common genre
  tic, not a target to imitate; if anything, tighten below this. Other fillers ("just,"
  "almost," "felt," "seemed") sit in the 5–25-per-10k band — moderate, not aggressively
  self-edited, not sloppy either.
- **Adverb density:** ~161 -ly adverbs per 10k words — fairly high; this is a book that
  lets adverbs carry tone rather than insisting on pure show-don't-tell. Not a craft flaw
  to correct, a genre-register signal to optionally match.
- **Dialogue-tag (reporting-verb) density:** ~41 per 10k words — moderate dialogue presence,
  consistent with an action/interiority-forward book rather than a dialogue-driven one.
- **Combat-vocabulary density:** ~22 per 10k words (strike/block/dodge/blade/impact/etc.) —
  present throughout, not just in set-piece fight chapters.
- **Readability:** Flesch Reading Ease 72.3, Flesch-Kincaid grade 6.8 — accessible YA/adult
  crossover register, short-to-medium sentence construction, low jargon-density outside the
  progression-vocabulary spikes.
- **Repetition discipline:** common connective 3-grams ("out of the," "one of the," "for a
  moment") sit at 2–4 per 10k words — normal English-prose background rate, not a
  distinctive tic to replicate. Don't chase these; they're noise, not signature.

---

## 9. One-paragraph brief for the drafting agent

> Write single-POV-dominant (~85–90% lead-character screen time), short-paragraph,
> pause-heavy prose (median paragraph ~18 words, scenes breaking roughly every 900–1000
> words). Mix short punch sentences (~28% of all sentences ≤5 words) against longer
> compound-complex ones (mean 14.6 words, wide spread) rather than settling into uniform
> mid-length sentences. Establish your progression system hard and often in the first
> third of the book, then let it recede to occasional high-stakes surfacing afterward. Land
> a protagonist growth/realization beat roughly every 2,200 words, spread evenly across the
> book rather than saved for the climax, and give 8–10 supporting characters small
> development beats each rather than concentrating growth in one deuteragonist. Moderate
> dialogue-tag and adverb use; accessible ~7th-grade reading level; combat vocabulary
> present in the background throughout, not confined to fight scenes. Every name, every
> system-label, every scene: yours, invented, original.

## MOVEMENT BRIEF — books/book-2680-national-measure/packets/MOVEMENT-001.md

# Movement 001 — The Second

> Built by TAC

- Series: Kindling 2680. Book Two, *The National Measure.* Public byline: Monroe Jackson.
- Seat: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 / O'Connor 1.3.0. Requested author: Claude Opus 5.5.
- Chapters: **1–8, one continuous creative run.** Open range. This is a brief, not eight
  chapter cards. Chapter count is fixed at eight; everything inside them is the author's.
- Working length: about 47,500–52,500 words, governed by complete scenes. No padding, no
  compression to fit a response. If limits intervene, save whole chapters and a precise
  checkpoint, and resume the same run.
- Read first, in full: Book One chapters 41–48;
  `books/book-2680-opening/provenance/MOVEMENT-006-CONTINUITY.md`; this book's
  `STATE_LEDGER.md`, `BOOK_MAP.md`, `CHARACTERS.md`, and `UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md`. Book One's prose
  governs every fact.
- Names: existing names are locked. Check `universe/NAME_REGISTRY.md` and search the
  manuscripts before inventing any minor name. Nothing from the research corpus.

## Where we enter

The evening of Sunday the 16th of the tenth month, 2680, is the last page of Book One. The
first free day is **Monday the 17th.** This movement may open on it or later; it ends by
**Saturday the 30th of the first month, 2681.** The months run thirty days: the eleventh
begins on a Monday, the twelfth on a Wednesday, the first month of 2681 on a **Friday.**

Kiva Rowan is seventeen, seated in Third Division as a catcher, unclassified, and on the Home
Line's roll. Her first two left fingers bend, grip, and report pressure, and nothing else.
The arm that went white to above the elbow has come back to where it was. **Healer Aske has
forbidden anything lit until the new year: not a step, not a ward for a tap.** She catches
with dark hands and her voice. She names her handoffs before she fumbles them. The disk is
cold. The dream has not come back.

Team Seven is fourth in the region and second step in its own right. Its book holds **25.53
drams, with 0.8 of plain ward-trace sealed as medical** and no way to make it up. No warrant
for Saltmere has been posted. Kellan has the Halden yard twice a week and is on Seven's book.
Perrin's ribs are strapped and his Hall's second team wants him from the new year. Anwen has
a summer place on the night van's ward line. Jude has 7.8 drams by name. Pru has her own
sheet.

Rhea Sorn's card is signed on its back by the four and goes back to her on **Monday the 1st
of the eleventh month:** catcher's second to Pike; two nights in the month from the first of
the new year, when the healer's card is up; dark hands; no door not on Kiva's certificate; Pru
for the cord; Team Seven first; until the last of the third month; either may say no; no seat
promised. A second sets nothing and calls nothing. She watches, and she says afterward what
the master did that he did not say aloud. She keeps her mouth shut until the bag is sealed.

The Ferren bank is out of step. **Seven drums (19, 20, 21, 25, 26, 27, 28) are still in,** to
be done one a day with the wardens' winter stock. Mara's reading hand was starved on the 6th
and eased; she may read a drum, and not a bank until the eleventh month. Her eleven sheets
are tied and wait on the dresser for the coast boat on Monday the 17th (Book One,
chapter 48), so they have not yet gone; `STATE_LEDGER.md` says "sent" and the manuscript
governs. Saltmere's harbour bank is still one engine.

**Orla Dane has Jessamy Hart's card and has shown it to nobody. Kiva does not know that he
has answered, that he fits pumps on the south coast, or that he has asked to read what she
writes.** Kiva's dream is known in full to Mara alone; the Chair knows only that one came.
The four, Pru, Noa, Rhea, Pike, Vane, and Aske do not know that she dreams.

## What this movement is for

It opens the book on work. A returning reader should be reoriented by what people do with
their hands in the first pages, and a recap paragraph should not be needed.

Its pressure: **for eleven weeks Kiva has no door, and then for a month she has doors and may
not use them where it counts.** Everybody round her is getting on. What is she for, dark? The
answer the movement earns is small and real. She becomes somebody who can see what a skilled
person does and say it so that a third person could do it. That is the root of everything
she builds later in this book. She should not know that yet.

Three things must be in practical view by the last page:

1. **The professional ceiling.** What a chartered catcher of twenty-six years does that no
   student does, and the night on which it is not enough.
2. **The damaged hand under new constraints.** Dark-hand work, a right-hand corner, a count
   that is somebody else's, and one honest test of what two dead fingers can still be asked.
3. **The National Measure as a bill and a date.** An entry to sign, a warrant to pay, a board
   that is a live hunt, and a four that is not yet good enough for it. The first board is
   Monday the 14th of the third month, forty-four days after this movement's last day; the
   road south is due by the end of the second month, thirty days on. `BOOK_MAP.md` gives
   Movement Two's entry pressure as "five weeks," which is loose. If the page counts, it
   counts truly.

## Consequences to land

These are the few fixed turns. Order, staging, and everything between them are the author's.

- **The card goes back signed,** on the 1st of the eleventh month.
- **Orla tells Kiva about Hart.** It is Orla's own choice, on the page, before the new year,
  and it is not dragged out of her. Kiva decides what a stranger may read. She sends method,
  in her own copy. **The dream does not go south,** and the scene should let the reader see
  her decide that. Orla reads Kiva's book weekly and does not know that Kiva dreams, so
  nothing copied or withheld may let Orla learn it; what Kiva keeps back is a line she
  chooses on the page.
- **Kiva's figures are read again** at the new year, for the first time since Classification
  Day, on the Circuit's instruments. Bounds: Draw falls a long way from 3.1 and is still poor
  beside a good single-door student; Ward Hold rises most, from the folding work; Stance
  rises a little from 6.2; Edge has hardly moved from 3.0. The rank frame holds **Flame** with
  one door declared and the others shut, and goes to dashes with two. There is no first door.
  She is not classified. Exact numbers are the author's and are then recorded. The two-door
  reading stays inside her certificate: one declared Ember-to-still crossing on a sanctioned
  mast, read by Noa for the office, which spends that week's go (about 0.30, off her own
  half dram). A stack read on the Chair's instruments is an incident. The figures show the
  summer's and autumn's work; the dark weeks earn none.
- **Each single door is relit under Aske's eye** before any crossing is thought of. No
  three-door sequence occurs in this movement. Nobody with authority asks for one.
- **Two nights on Graywater's van,** both in the first month. The first is a learning night.
  On the second, the book answer fails against an expression that mixes two behaviors the
  crews know separately, and a professional pays for it. The plan expects that to be Pike,
  and expects his hands. The author may find a truer cost, so long as the ceiling is shown
  and carried forward.
- **Kiva begins writing down Pike's craft** in seven columns, because he asked a second to
  tell him what he did not say. He should dislike some of what he reads.
- **Kellan chooses his four.** The National lets a fighter stand for one four only, declared
  at entry. The plan expects Halden License, with his name left on Seven's book for calls,
  and expects the choice to be his and to cost him something at the Rowan table.
- **Seven's entry is signed under a captain.** The plan expects Perrin, who keeps the word and
  the brake, and expects him to mind.
- **The warrant is posted** at more than Kellan's ten: planned at three drams a head, five
  heads, fifteen. Which five depends on Kellan's choice; with him gone they are Kiva, Anwen,
  Perrin, Jude, and Pru as reserve. Fifteen from 25.53 leaves 10.53 before any training, and
  the hand-to-Ember crossings and Coombe's floors already planned against it (about 1.8 and
  4.0, `BOOK_MAP.md` section 8) leave 4.73 before any call reserve. The book does not run dry
  in this movement; it must be shown that its "something over" is already spoken for. Whether
  the warrant is paid or only posted here is the author's. If paid, enter it and keep the
  sum exact to the hundredth.
- **Saltmere answers Mara.** They tried the eleven sheets and failed. The harbour's water
  does not lie still. It should arrive as a fact from working people, with a detail Mara can
  use.
- **There is no plain ward-trace to be had.** The reserve stays at 0.8 unless it is opened
  for a shown medical need and entered so.

## Action and learning this movement owes

Developed scenes, each a changing problem. No montage of eleven weeks.

- **Drum work,** early, with dark hands. A drum is a small bank: one cell alone on an open
  link. Let something about one of the seven refuse the method that worked on the hill, so
  that the reader learns the method again by watching it bend. Mara is in charge and has one
  hand on a card. Kiva is not Bench-passed and touches no municipal cell; Mara's rule from
  Book One stands (she does not come over a sill). Her use at a drum is the ground she
  reads and the count she names, from the pavement.
- **A dark-hands floor or yard with the four,** in which Kiva's usefulness is in question and
  Pru's is not.
- **The first van night:** terrain, positions, the *before* column read out, Pike's hem, and
  what a second can and cannot see from her knees.
- **The second van night:** the major set piece. It may take 2,000 words or more. Set the
  ground, the reach, the roles, and the limits privately before writing it. The thing they
  meet obeys spawn anatomy and combines it. The crew tests, meets resistance, adjusts, and
  pays. Kiva is forbidden to set or call. What she does inside that rule, or at its edge, is
  the scene's question, and its consequence carries into Movement Two. Choose the
  combination so that it is Seven's first, failed meeting with a behavior Movement Two
  trains against: a pack that keeps a count among itself, or a hem that folds as a catcher's
  ward does (`BOOK_MAP.md` section 6). The crew need not name it. If Pike's hands are the
  cost, the card's wording stands (second to Pike, two nights in each of the second and
  third months, six at most), so decide and record what his hands do to those four nights.
- **One honest test of the two fingers.** They report pressure. Hart knew a stepped bank "with
  the hand, the bad one." The planned finding is narrow: through something solid, they may
  tell her that a thing is keeping time, and not what time. It is not healing, not a combat
  sense, and not reliable. It rests on what Book One gave them: "something's pushing" is
  their word, and the heel and last two fingers did the reading in chapter 41 while the first
  two said only "pavement." So test the two alone, against a steady push and a counted one,
  and let them fail the way they fail. If the page does not earn it, leave the question open
  and say so in the checkpoint.
- **A human contest** somewhere in the eight: Seven against people who adapt, with Kiva on one
  declared door after the new year, and a correction the four can try again. It is not the
  Halden License learning fight; that is Movement Two's, with Kellan opposite, and is spent
  there. A yard or practice contest needs no ruling. If it is a sanctioned event, the
  Chair's 2681 conditions are on no page and must be written on this one (one door declared,
  any change declared).

Carry every injury, strap, card, and dram forward. Perrin's ribs take weeks. Mara's hand has a
date on it.

## Formula, applied to this movement

The owner's numerical formula governs. Write toward it across the eight chapters, then measure.

- **Viewpoint:** about 87% Kiva. About 6,200–6,800 words of cutaway across three or four of
  the five lenses (Anwen, Kellan, Mara, Orla, Rhea), each for something Kiva cannot see.
  Mara with the coast's answer and Orla deciding to tell are the natural two. Book One
  chapter 48 already gave Orla reading Hart's card alone; do not give it twice.
- **Progression vocabulary:** this is the front third. The formula's 58 hits per 10,000 words
  and 1.7 ratio work out to about **80 per 10,000 here** and about 47 after Movement Two.
  Teach hard, through attempts: the license steps, the national roll, the boards and how they
  are marked, the warrant, Draw and Hold and what the re-reading means, a second's duties,
  the slate's six columns, plain against stepped ash, yield and grade and resonance.
- **Development:** a Kiva realization or decision about every 2,200 words, so twenty-one to
  twenty-four in the movement. Small beats for at least eight others; Pike, Pru, Orla, Mara,
  Kellan, and Perrin are owed most here.
- **Sentences:** mean 14.6 words; median 11; about 27.7% at five words or fewer; about 3.3%
  at forty or more. Short beats beside long ones, not a steady middle.
- **Paragraphs and scenes:** median paragraph about 18 words; scenes near 950 words; about 8.7
  breaks per 10,000 words. These are directional proxies.
- **Readability:** Flesch Reading Ease near 72.3; grade near 6.8.
- Section 8's filler, adverb, and tag figures are secondary. Do not chase them.

Book One's Movement One missed the sentence targets on the first pass because the dialogue
ran clipped. Let narration carry the longer sentences here from the start.

## Protected boundaries

- **Nothing lit by Kiva before Friday the 1st of the first month, 2681,** and then only as
  Aske's card allows. Dark hands on Rhea's floor all movement.
- **No door on Rhea's floor that is not on Kiva's certificate.** No crossing in the field.
- **Three doors: three clean, not asked, not done.**
- **The fingers do not improve.** The arm is as it was.
- **The disk is cold** and does nothing. A fourth-column line is owed only after a crossing.
- **The dream does not recur in this movement.** The words "Unroofed Sky" do not appear. No
  one new learns that Kiva dreams, except by a decision of hers made on the page; the plan
  expects none.
- **Hart** is a card, a hand, and a trade. He is not met. He does not forgive.
- **Saltmere** is not reached. No Saltmere competitor, Glory master, or Board officer comes on
  stage except by letter, wire, or notice. Dinah Coombe may be announced and not met.
- **No one explains** the Step, the hold, the notch, the lamps, the filter, the plate, or why
  nothing is plain. People may count, compare, and be wrong.
- **Students close no tear** and cut only under a foreman's slate.
- **The ledger is exact.** Every dram spent, lent, earned, or refused is entered and adds.
  The red bags are evidence. No free practice ash.
- **Nobody is a villain,** including Halden, the Works, the Board, and Rhea Sorn.
- **Selka Dray** is in a chair, mending, unpersuaded.
- No commit, push, publication, narration, review, scoring, or repair inside the run.

## What the author owns

Where in the winter the book opens. How eleven dark weeks are weighted against one lit month.
Which drum misbehaves, and how. The van's ground, the crew's talk, the thing on the second
night and what the crew ends up calling it. Whether Pike is the one hurt. What Kiva does at
the edge of a second's rule. The re-reading's exact figures. How Orla says it. What Kiva
copies out for Hart and what she finds she cannot. The Rowan table when Kellan chooses.
Perrin as captain. Humor, weather, food, the laundry next door, and every line of talk. Where
the eight chapters break. The last image.

Use movement-sized causality, and let the questions get dearer as they widen: first private
(what is she for, dark, on a count that is not hers); then professional (what a master does
that he cannot say, and where it stops); then institutional (the bill, the captain, the four
who must be chosen). Each stage should make the next cost more. Scene order inside that
ladder is the author's. The dark weeks should teach the thing the van nights test. The
second night should change what the four must train for. The warrant should make that
training a matter of ash. The last chapter should leave Seven facing Movement Two with a
date, a bill, a captain, and a catcher who has started to write somebody else's method down.

## Output and stop condition

Write complete chapters to:

- `manuscript/chapter-01.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-02.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-03.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-04.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-05.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-06.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-07.md`
- `manuscript/chapter-08.md`

Then write `provenance/MOVEMENT-001-CONTINUITY.md`; append the actual model, session, prompt
hash, word counts, viewpoint counts, and edition status to `AUTHORSHIP.md`; and add a Movement
One block to `STATE_LEDGER.md` from surviving prose only. Record every choice that departs
from this brief or from `BOOK_MAP.md`. Stop after the complete first draft and its records.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-national-measure/OWNER_BRIEF.md

# Kindling 2680 — Book Two owner brief

Working title: **The National Measure**. Public series: **Kindling 2680**. Public byline:
**Monroe Jackson**. This is Book Two, not a restart or alternate edition.

Use the accepted ending and state of Book One exactly. The new novel begins after Kiva
Rowan's first collegiate season, with the National Measure at Saltmere in the third month of
2681 approaching. Kiva remains the dominant protagonist. Keep her team, family, teachers,
rivals, and professional contacts as people with independent wants rather than replacing the
cast with a new academy ensemble.

## Reader promise

- Preserve the Iron-Prince-like pleasure the owner wants at the level of mechanism: visible
  training, meaningful corrections, collegiate competition, standings, rivals who adapt,
  professional rift work, and earned progression. Use only this world's original people,
  terminology, institutions, powers, events, and phrasing.
- Widen the ladder from Meridian's regional circuit to Saltmere's National Measure,
  intercity schools, licensed hunts, professional recruitment, and industrial Glory houses.
- Give Kiva several connected chapters of training for stronger and more varied Riftspawn.
  Fighting and containment earn finite ash; ash funds the next threshold but never replaces
  learned soul control.
- Let filter thinning increase both human potential and spawn variety/strength. Early public
  explanations may call it a gifted generation and a bad rift season, but the evidence must
  accumulate fairly.
- Advance the Unroofed Sky through independent human evidence. By the ending, more than one
  Glory user or historical record must establish that the dream is shared, while its cause
  and full meaning remain reserved.
- Deliver a complete Book Two arc with a satisfying national-scale ending. Do not write a
  bridge novel whose only purpose is Book Three.

## Canon locks from Book One

- Kiva is seventeen, a three-door catcher, seated in Third Division and entered on the Home
  Line roll. She is still unclassified and uncertified beyond the exact conditions in the
  Book One ledger. She is not secretly omnipotent.
- Her left arm and first two fingers retain the established damage. Nothing silently heals
  them. At the Book One ending, Aske forbids her from lighting until the new year.
- Kiva has three clean combined sequences in total: yard, Pit, Old Yard. A fourth use must be
  earned, bounded, and costly.
- The Satori disk is an appointed historical object, not a bloodline prize or an unexplained
  problem-solver. It was cold throughout Book One. Its deeper origin remains reserved.
- Team Seven remains Kiva, Kellan Renn, Anwen Pryce, and Perrin Cade. Kellan has the Halden
  yard while staying on Seven's book. Pru Finch has her own sheet and works the cord under the
  exact terms already recorded.
- Orla Dane remains Kiva's accountable sponsor and teacher. Mara Rowan remains her aunt,
  guardian, and practical home stake. Rhea Sorn's bounded night-van offer runs from the new
  year through the end of the third month: catcher's second to Pike, two nights a month,
  dark hands, no door outside Kiva's certificate, Pru on the cord, Team Seven first, either
  party may say no, and no seat promised.
- The Ferren bank is out of step. Seven drums remain, one to be handled per day. Saltmere's
  thirty-six-cell harbour bank went into step in the same minute and has not come out.
- Mara's hand, Perrin's ribs, Team Seven's ash book, the final Regional placements, and every
  other body/resource state carry forward from the accepted ledger. Costs are not reset
  between books.
- Jessamy Hart's card remains private to Orla at Book One's end. Kiva does not know about the
  south-coast pump-fitter or his request to read her work.
- Kiva's dream is known only by the exact people recorded in the ledger. Do not spread that
  knowledge casually.

## Book shape

- Six open-range movements of eight chapters each, chapters 1–48, approximately
  285,000–315,000 words unless the scenes earn more.
- Approximately 87% Kiva viewpoint/focus, with the remaining purposeful cutaways spread
  across four or five established or newly necessary characters.
- Begin with action, work, or a consequential choice rather than a recap lecture. Reorient a
  returning reader through what the cast does.
- Movement One should use the winter preparation and Rhea's night-van arrangement to teach
  the professional-rift ceiling, test Kiva's damaged hand under new constraints, and put the
  National Measure in immediate practical view. Its eight chapters must form one continuous
  creative run with substantial scenes, not eight summaries.
- Keep the familiar cast central while adding only the Saltmere competitors, officials,
  professionals, industry figures, and Glory witnesses the book actually needs. New names
  must avoid existing series collisions.

## Reserved for later books

- Do not completely remove the planetary filter in Book Two.
- Do not deliver pure Glory presence worldwide.
- Do not explain the ultimate cause of the Fall, the final origin/function of the disk, or a
  single final doctrine that makes all schools obsolete.
- Do not resolve international convergence. Book Two may prove that it has begun.

Plan the full novel before drafting. Then prepare Movement One as an open-range eight-chapter
packet. Do not write prose during the planning call.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-national-measure/STATE_LEDGER.md

# Kindling 2680, Book Two — state ledger

> Built by TAC

Status: **Book Two entry state only. No Book Two prose exists.** Everything in sections 1–9
is the accepted Book One exit state (evening of Sunday the 16th of the tenth month, 2680),
carried from `books/book-2680-opening/STATE_LEDGER.md`,
`books/book-2680-opening/provenance/MOVEMENT-006-CONTINUITY.md`, and chapters 47–48. Where
this file and the Book One manuscript disagree, the Book One manuscript governs. Section 10
records the checks made before this file was saved. Section 11 lists what was reconciled.
Movement blocks are appended below section 11 only from surviving Book Two prose.

## 1. Calendar

Every month has thirty days and twelve months make the year (Aske's "eleven weeks and four
days" from Monday the 10th of the tenth month to the new year confirms that no day stands
between the twelfth month and the first). A month therefore begins two weekdays after the
one before it.

| Month | 1st falls on | 30th falls on | Fixed points |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tenth, 2680 | Saturday | Sunday | Book One ends Sunday the 16th. First free day of Book Two: **Monday the 17th.** |
| Eleventh, 2680 | Monday | Tuesday | **The 1st:** Rhea Sorn's card goes back to her, signed or torn. Mara's hand is on a card "until the eleventh month." |
| Twelfth, 2680 | Wednesday | Thursday | Nothing fixed. |
| First, 2681 | **Friday** | Saturday | **The 1st:** Aske's ban on anything lit ends, if he signs the card up. The night van offer begins. Perrin joins his Hall's second team. |
| Second, 2681 | Sunday | Monday | Nothing fixed. |
| Third, 2681 | **Tuesday** | **Wednesday** | **The National Measure, at Saltmere.** Selka Dray "on her feet by the third month." Rhea Sorn's card ends on the 30th. The 11th (a Friday) is seventy-seven years to the day after the Ferren keeper's fever entry of 2604. |
| Fourth, 2681 | Thursday | Friday | Nothing fixed. |

Earlier anchors that prove the chain: the fifth month's 29th was a Wednesday (the Pit); the
sixth began on a Friday; the seventh on a Sunday; the ninth on a Thursday (the Regional, Friday
the 16th and Saturday the 17th); the tenth on a Saturday.

## 2. Kiva Rowan

- **Age and household.** Seventeen. Lives over Rowan Instruments with her aunt and guardian,
  Mara Rowan. A guardian's hand is still wanted beside her own wherever a rule says "under
  eighteen." No birthday is fixed in the supplied record; Book Two does not move her age
  unless the Book One manuscript gives a date.
- **Place.** *Rowan, K., Third Division, the Crooked Line: seated. No longer provisional.
  Catcher.* (the Chair, the 14th of the tenth month). Crooked Line third team; Team Seven.
  "Her sponsor's line and every condition on her certificate stand as written. She is not
  thereby classified." There is still no column for her; there is a page headed *Witnessed*
  with two lines on it.
- **The Home Line.** *Rowan, K. Entered on the Line's roll as catcher, autumn 2680. Hand as
  before.* Not a seat. No wage but a line-hand's day rate when called. Called by a Line
  foreman, with her sponsor's leave while she has a sponsor, "to set a mark, to read ground,
  to call a count that is hers and name the ones that are not." She has not yet been called.
- **Certificate, as it stands.**
  - Orla Dane's line: recovery only, dark hands; **and one crossing, Ember to still, low, to a
    mark, declared to a count, on a sanctioned mast read by the Chair's reader, one in seven
    days.** Not certified: any stack, any third door, anything Orla has not seen six times.
  - The Chair's conditions of the spring: one door declared to the referee before every
    sanctioned event, and any change declared; her book to her sponsor weekly; no live-cell
    events until her sponsor certifies in writing; one sanctioned-event incident ends her
    season. (The 2680 season is read. Whether that last line is re-issued for 2681 is the
    Chair's to say and is not on any page.)
  - The Chair's conditions of the 2nd of the seventh month: no second door on any Circuit
    floor except a crossing declared to a count and read at the time from a sanctioned mast
    by the office's reader; a stack so read is an incident; not at the Bench; recovery only on
    any floor with a contained rift. No crossing counts unless the tape shows the cliff. Noa
    Bexley is reader to the office.
  - **Selka Dray's mast rule** (any candidate with more than one door is read from a
    sanctioned mast by a reader of the Meridian office) has gone to Saltmere under seal.
- **Crossings on the record.** Ember to still: one under a field direction (the 29th of the
  fifth month), then Orla's six on sanctioned masts, and one begun and not landed in a lane
  at the Regional. Hand to Ember: landed once (the eighth month, 0.30). A crossing costs
  about 0.30 to 0.31 of a dram, of which about 0.26 is the gap. An unlanded crossing still
  spends the hand and the week's go. A stack is a miss, forbidden, and an incident if read.
- **Three doors.** Order: hand, Ember, still ("catch, carry, plant"); 0.59 whole. **Three
  clean in all: a yard (the 29th of the eighth month), the Pit (the 17th of the ninth), the
  Old Yard (the 5th of the tenth).** Uncertified, unclassified, and by the Chair's written
  entry "not to be asked of her." The last two were done under a recorded Home Line direction
  and an officer's hand, with the price said first by her healer; the last was offered and not
  asked. Never twice inside a fortnight. Aske does not know that the arm would come back a
  fourth time.
- **Readings.** Nothing has been measured since Classification Day, 2680: Draw 3.1; Hold,
  Edge 3.0, Ward 2.6, Stance 6.2; rank unsettled (the frame flickers); first door, none; the
  Call column dark. **No rank, Hold, Draw or bench figure has been re-measured.**
- **Body.**
  - **The first two fingers of the left hand have permanently lost fine sensation.** They
    bend, grip, and report pressure ("something's pushing"). They do not tell edge from flat,
    hot from cold, or one thread from two. Ward-trace ash and rest are ruled out as remedies.
  - The left arm lost feeling to a hand above the elbow on the 5th of the tenth month and came
    back over four days "to where it was, and not a hair less."
  - **Aske's card of the 10th: nothing lit until the new year. "Not a step. Not a ward for a
    tap." Dark hands and her voice.** His standing rule before that: one crossing in seven
    days, with the hand seen the next morning; the three-door is not on his card at all.
  - The left knee is still strapped and complained once, running. The three heat-lines run
    from the breastbone over the collarbone toward the left shoulder and upper arm.
- **Handoffs she names** (the list of the 30th of the ninth month): the cord, all three turns
  (Pru Finch); the slack of a hem on her left side (she takes a right-hand corner or is called
  to); tin lids with a lip; the pin of a seal and whether wax has gone off (Anwen has the
  wax); a button and a cuff; **the count of a thing that keeps time (somebody else's, named
  before).**
- **The disk.** Cold. It warmed once, on the 29th of the fifth month, after her first correct
  deliberate crossing, and has been cold through every crossing and all three three-door
  sequences since. Recognized by the Compact Library's Reader as the plate of nine rings
  carried by the first teachers and *released* by appointment. Held of the Line; borne by K.
  Rowan; not equipment, an instrument, or evidence; no hand but the bearer's without a vote of
  the watch; the bearer writes in the fourth column within the week after any crossing. What
  it is for is unknown. The first bearer's book is lost.
- **The dream.** No recurrence since Classification Day, 2680. No fever on any night of the
  autumn. She has Annick's phrase *the sky unroofed* and the Ferren keeper's line of 2604.
  **The name "the Unroofed Sky" has never appeared on the page.**
- **Her book.** Green cover, soft at the corners; a spring, a summer, and an autumn; no page
  ever torn out. It goes to Orla weekly and lies on the kitchen table nightly for Mara.

## 3. Team Seven

- **The four:** Kiva Rowan (catcher), Anwen Pryce (Hold), Perrin Cade (carry; calls *off*
  and keeps the brake), Kellan Renn (blade). **Jude Talley** stands as Kill by name, on the
  line for a head's share and not on the book. **Pru Finch** is a catcher with a sheet of her
  own and the standing reserve.
- **Standing.** Fourth at the Regional Measure. **Second step, in its own right;** the
  brackets came off on the 14th of the tenth month. It goes forward to the National Measure.
- **The book, signed by four on Saturday the 15th of the tenth month:**

| Line | Drams |
|---|---:|
| **Held** | **25.53** |
| **Sealed medical (W, plain ward-trace)** | **0.8** |
| By name to J. Talley, in one autumn | 7.8 |

  Check: 23.62 − 3.0 (second purse sheet and poles, for Pru) − 0.70 (call drams spent) − 0.59
  (three-door line) + 7.2 (to the book from Stopgate's pot) = 26.53; less 1.0 returned at par
  to the wardens of Stopgate = **25.53.** The loan of 4.0 to the casualty reserve went out and
  came back. Sealed: 2.0 − 1.2 (Mara Rowan's reading hand, entered as medical) = **0.8.**
- **The reserve cannot be made up.** There is no plain ward-trace to be bought in Meridian;
  every can has "had the hair" since the thaw. It "stays eight-tenths until somebody kills
  something quiet."
- **Not on the team's book:** Kiva's own weekly half dram; Pru's pay, which is off the
  Circuit's reserve card.
- **Red, and untouched:** Seven's own rung from the Pit, stepped, on Kiva's tag; Pru's bag
  from Stopgate on her own tag. Across the district 111 and 503.7 drams stand red-tagged from
  before, with 560.4 more from Stopgate. Where the red row goes is not said. The patent claim
  on the Weighbridge bag is before the Compact's court, unruled.
- **Saltmere.** **No warrant has been posted or entered.** Kellan reckons two drams a head at
  the least, five heads, ten drams. Nobody from the Circuit has been sent to the coast in
  eleven years.
- **Open inside the four:** which four Kellan stands with at Saltmere. He cut for the Halden
  license four at the Regional and declared for Seven under rule nineteen once the floor was
  struck. He counts himself among "five heads" on the 15th. No page settles it.

## 4. The offer

Rhea Sorn's card, slate blue, read in full to the four and signed on its back by all four on
Saturday the 15th of the tenth month:

> *Rowan, K. Catcher's second, to Pike. The night van, two nights in the month, from the first
> of the new year, when her healer's card is up. Dark hands. No door on my floor that is not
> on her certificate. Finch with her, for the cord, if Finch will. If T7 is called on a night
> of mine, she is T7's and I go short. Until the last of the third month. Then we look again,
> and either of us may say no. No seat is promised. R.S.*

- Kiva's two conditions, accepted: the *before* column is read out each night to all; her four
  have the first word.
- Pru Finch has said yes, on being asked by Kiva as well.
- A catcher's second "sets nothing. She calls nothing." She kneels at Pike's other hem and
  tells him afterward what he did that he did not say aloud. She is not to open her mouth
  until the bag is sealed.
- At most **six nights:** two in each of the first, second, and third months of 2681.
- The card is to go back to Rhea on **Monday the 1st of the eleventh month.**
- Rhea's slate has six columns: *Before. Closed. Lives. Works. Tinned. Remarks.*

## 5. Other people, as they stand

| Person | Body, place, and open thread |
|---|---|
| **Mara Rowan** | Grade Two, restored. Reader of the trunk for Examiner Rusk; has the hill, the bank, and the stopping of it. **Left (reading) hand starved to the wrist on the 6th and eased inside the hour: a week before she reads a drum, a month before she reads a bank.** The right hand has told her nothing since Linden Row (2677). Eleven sheets in seven columns, *How the Ferren bank was taken out of step, with what was got wrong first,* are tied and waiting on the dresser for the coast boat on Monday the 17th. Her page of the 29th (Kiva's dream as told) is sealed and timed in the Library's press. The Order's six questions are in her hands; two are to be answered. |
| **Orla Dane** | Accountable sponsor; her standing rides on Kiva's record. Holds **Jessamy Hart's card** and has shown it to nobody. Moved her crate a pace north. Bad left knee, stick. |
| **Anwen Pryce** | Has the wax and the sealed reserve. A relief's place on Doran Mace's ward line (the night van) for next summer; Mace left her name where it was (*Said so as she went*). Has told her mother. "Done minding." |
| **Perrin Cade** | **Two ribs sprung, not broken; strapped.** 380 kilos for thirty counts, cut in the stone. His Hall's second team from the new year. Minds, and says so. |
| **Kellan Renn** | Has the Halden yard, Tuesdays and Thursdays, nothing owed. On Seven's book "as a blade who's told where he's wanted." His mother signs "A. Renn, grader." |
| **Jude Talley** | Kill by name; 7.8 drams by name. Will stand at Saltmere, asked by name. Wants to see the sea. |
| **Pru Finch** | Her own sheet, her own red tag, the cord. Nipped to the first joint once. Goes to the night van with Kiva. |
| **Noa Bexley** | Reader to the Chair's office. His page reads *Not yet weighed* and *Not one thing.* Three columns before the Charter Board, Mr. Adlam second. |
| **Selka Dray** | Left leg broken above the knee and burned with cold on the 17th of the ninth month; in a chair; "on my feet by the third month." Unpersuaded, on the record twice. Means to beat Kiva in a lane at Saltmere, on a declared count, with a needle on her. Not a friend. |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Graywater. Wrote to the board; the brackets came off. Burned a card that said *mine.* |
| **Pike** | Graywater's catcher, six-and-twenty years. Will have Kiva as his second. |
| **Nairn** | Graywater's second blade; the leg healed; walking without the stick. |
| **Hollis Vane** | Home Line foreman ("the Sag"). Three directions to Kiva in his ledger, two with a cost column. |
| **Ysra Crest** | Placement Chair. Posted "seated." Keeps the *Witnessed* page. |
| **Healer Aske** | Keeps Kiva's card. Would not round the price down. |
| **Lowen Brask** | Old Four; named the hold and the paid-out string; two to the bar. |
| **Examiner Rusk, Mr. Adlam, Lumb, Ansty, A. Renn, Officer Yeo** | As in the Movement Six checkpoint. Yeo went south on the 8th and will ask for the Works' reader again in the spring. |

## 6. The grid and the rifts

- **The Ferren bank** (twenty-four cells, the Municipal Works') **is out of step.** The method:
  nothing in the water that keeps time; pumps stopped; every cell quenched on one word; fresh
  cans, each salted with a pinch of plain ash that "has never heard a beat"; a card with its
  own count under each handle, "not two alike in this room"; a reader between each cell and
  the next; each pump restarted on its own eight cells. It needs the main to be **still.**
- **Seven drums remain in step** on the Brickfields trunk: **19, 20, 21, 25, 26, 27, 28,** each
  alone on an open link, to be done one a day with the wardens' old winter stock. None has been
  done on the page.
- **Drum twenty-nine is fouled** (about thirty drams through a split plank). Its four streets
  are on boxes for about a month from the 6th of the tenth month.
- The Stopgate sluice works. The Crooked Line School's core house never fell in.
- **Saltmere's harbour bank, thirty-six cells, went into step at twenty past two on the 17th of
  the ninth month and has not come out.** Its pumps have not dropped a stroke. Nobody at
  Saltmere knows how to take a bank out.
- **Nobody has said** that water can step a cell, why a tear throws stepped ash, why husks
  stood a hold, or why the bank fell in on the 17th.
- The Home Line's suspension of held tears has not been lifted on the page.
- A can of civic stock in the Works' own store was cut with soot. Noticed; nobody has taken
  it up.
- **Evidence.** The notch on Kiva's pen falls eleven seconds after her second cliff (three
  occasions). The lamps' T-0 falls when a hold comes down. They are two things. T-0 entries
  on record: 2446, 2604, 2676, and three in 2680 (the Pit twice, Stopgate once). Noa's three
  columns (people; rifts and spawn; Saltmere) all rise, faster since the fifth month. One
  unvouched line says an overseas port's bank fell in at the same hour.

## 7. Knowledge boundaries

| Fact | Who has it | Who does not |
|---|---|---|
| **Kiva has dreamed, and what she saw** | Kiva; **Mara** (told in full; her page is sealed) | Everyone else |
| That a dream came on Classification Day, with no content | **Ysra Crest** | — |
| Annick's fever entry, read in the room | Kiva (the sixth month); Orla (2664). Neither has told the other what it says. Orla has not been told that Kiva dreams. | Mara has not read it |
| That Kiva's lips moved, counting, as she read Annick | The sister of the Even Hand; the Halden endowment's observer | — |
| The Ferren keeper's entry of 2604 | Kiva, Mara, Lumb (as a page in his book) | Aske, A. Renn, Lumb, and Mr. Adlam were out of earshot and **do not know that Kiva has dreamed** |
| The four T-0 entries, the 2446 margin, the disputed box, Orla's 2664 reading | Anwen, Kellan, Perrin, Pru, Noa | **None of them knows of the dream** |
| **Jessamy Hart's card and his request to read Kiva's work** | **Orla Dane only** | **Kiva.** Also Mara, the four, the Chair |
| That Hart is a pump-fitter on the south coast whose harbour bank stepped | Orla | Kiva |
| A color in the dream with no word for it | Kiva alone; on no page | Mara |
| What the bearer's book records of Mara's refusal in 2669 | Kiva, Vane | Mara, as far as the page shows |
| What the disk is for; the first book | Nobody | — |

## 8. Institutions

- The Regional result stands without brackets: **1. Halden License. 2. Old Four. 3. Team
  Twenty-Eight. 4. T7.** "The first company goes forward to the National Measure, at Saltmere,
  in the third month, 2681. Entries under each team's own hand."
- Circuit rules made in 2680 and still in force: a gate shall be left lit; any catcher may
  red-tag a bag on stated grounds; no ash above I-trace on any Circuit floor; every Measure
  dram combed at the gate.
- The Charter Board has three sheets under the Chair's seal. It has not answered.
- The Order of the Even Hand's offer of a year as a guest, one door, is "on the latch."
- The Crooked Line School's tenth edition restores the lane in seven columns, with Kiva's
  lines in the fourth and sixth.

## 9. Unresolved threads carried in

The cause of the Step. Why a hold stood. What the notch and the lamps are. Who else has
dreamed. The filter. The disk's purpose and first book. Kiva's classification. Whether three
doors can be made safe, taught, or done a fourth time. Saltmere's bank. The seven drums. The
red bags and the court claim. The cut can. The Charter Board. Hart. Dray. Rhea's "we look
again." Kellan's four. The warrant. The reserve that cannot be made up. Mara's sealed page.
The overseas line.

## 10. Checks made before saving (planning session, 2026-10-12)

| Item | Source checked | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Month chain | Checkpoint §1 and §9 item 21; ledger blocks for Movements Three to Six; Aske's count in chapter 47 | Consistent. The first month of 2681 begins on a Friday; the third on a Tuesday; the 30th of the third month is a Wednesday. |
| Certification | Ledger, Movement Five block; checkpoint §2; the board in chapter 48 | Seated, unclassified; one certified crossing (Ember to still); hand to Ember not certified; three-door not certified and "not to be asked." |
| Body limits | Chapter 47 (Aske, Monday the 10th); checkpoint §2 | Two fingers permanent; arm returned; nothing lit until the new year. |
| Team Seven's book | Checkpoint §6 table and check line; chapter 48 | Held 25.53; sealed 0.8; Talley 7.8. Arithmetic re-run and correct. |
| Rhea Sorn's offer | Chapter 48, card quoted in full | Quoted above verbatim. Six nights at most. No seat. |
| Hart's knowledge boundary | Chapter 48 (Orla's cutaway and the closing survey); checkpoint §0 and §4 | Private to Orla. Kiva has not been told. |
| National Measure timing | The board in chapter 48 | Saltmere, the third month, 2681. No day is given; Book Two sets the days. |

## 11. Reconciled for Book Two

1. **Hart's hand.** Orla says in chapter 8 that he "lost a hand" and left "with one hand."
   His card says "the hand, the bad one." Both stand: Orla never saw him afterward. He has one
   working hand and one ruined one that is still on his arm.
2. **Who dreams.** The series bible says that "a small number of high-rank practitioners"
   record the fragments. Book One's second account is a pump keeper's. The accepted book
   governs: the fever reaches ordinary people who sit beside banks and lamps; high-rank
   accounts are fuller. Book Two's plan builds on both.
3. **The notch.** The Movement Three block's "about forty seconds" was the keeper's guess and
   is superseded: eleven seconds.
4. **Kellan's "five heads."** Left open by Book One and settled inside Book Two (see
   `BOOK_MAP.md`).
5. **The seven drums** are 19, 20, 21, 25, 26, 27, and 28. Twenty-nine is fouled, not stepped.
