## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-national-measure/BOOK_MAP.md

# Kindling 2680, Book Two — *The National Measure* — book map

> Built by TAC

Status: **planned, not drafted.** This map states intended change, pressure, and payoff. It is
not a set of chapter contracts. The author owns scene selection, chapter boundaries, tactics,
conversation, humor, and the route through each movement. Entry state is `STATE_LEDGER.md`.
Everything here is `PLANNED` until surviving prose makes it canon.

## 1. Book promise

Kiva Rowan ended her first collegiate season seated, unclassified, with eight fingers that
report and a company's card that promises no seat. Book Two takes her two hundred miles to the
coast. She spends a winter with her hands dark, kneeling at a professional's hem with her
mouth shut. She trains for Riftspawn that no longer die by the book. She stands the National
Measure at Saltmere among twenty-four fours and schools that solved her problem a different
way. And she finds that the thing Meridian did once, by hand, to one bank on one hill, has to
be done on water that never lies still.

The title carries three meanings, and the book pays all three: the competition; the nation
counting what it has (keepers' books, banks, fevers, plain ash); and **the beat itself**, the
count of four that more and more of the country's ash is keeping.

## 2. The argument

Book One asked why the post is crooked and answered: keep the reason with the method. Book
Two asks what happens when a method has to **travel.** Mara's eleven sheets reach the coast and
fail there, because a harbour is not a storm drain. Kiva's three-door sequence cannot travel
at all; it lives in one damaged hand. What can travel is the part she has been made to learn
all winter: say what the piece is, who has it, and when it changes hands.

Her earned capability in this book is not a fourth three-door. It is the **handed crossing**
(working term): catch, carry, and plant done by three people with three unlike doors, with
one caller who has been inside both gaps and can say when the load passes. It costs almost no
ash. It costs trust, rehearsal, and a count that is not hers.

## 3. Complete ending

**The Measure is read in full.** No board is struck. The Ropewalk School's first four wins the
National Measure. Halden License is second. **Team Seven finishes seventh of twenty-four and
makes the national license roll, which takes eight, by one mark.** Seven wins one board
outright, the Carry, on Perrin Cade's back. Selka Dray beats Kiva in a lane, on a declared
count, with a needle on her, exactly as promised; Seven wins the lane on the other three.

Kiva loses that exchange by choice. One crossing in seven days is all her card allows, and by
then she knows what the week's crossing is for. She fights Dray on one door and says why
beforehand, to Dray's face.

**The dream becomes a public fact.** Before the Charter Board, Kiva has Mara's sealed page
opened: timed and sealed before she ever read Annick, it proves that her account was her own.
Beside it lie Annick's entry, the Ferren keeper's line of 2604, the Saltmere harbour keeper's
line for the same night, and the first returns of a national reading of remarks columns. Two
living Glory masters then say under their own names that they have dreamed it: Clemency
Thrale, Master of the Order of the Even Hand, and Ambrose Halden, the Proprietor, who was
alive on the night in 2604. A Board clerk borrows Annick's phrase, and the papers print **the
Unroofed Sky.** Nobody can say what it means. The two masters read it in opposite ways.

**The Slack.** At the dawn low-water slack of Wednesday the 23rd of the third month, a mixed
cohort from the Measure's roll and three professional crews take Saltmere's harbour bank of
thirty-six cells out of step. The water has to be still, and harbour water is still for about
twenty minutes. The thing standing in the basin has to come off the water first. It is the
first Barrel-class expression on the page, and it is keeping the harbour's time.

- **Students** stand the wall, carry, cut on a foreman's slate, and bag. Kiva calls three
  handed crossings in a row and tests every mark first.
- **When a link fails,** she supplies it herself: one certified crossing, on a sanctioned mast,
  read by the office's reader. It is the first decisive thing she has ever done that was
  entirely inside her certificate. **When a second link fails,** she makes a second crossing in
  the same hour, against her healer's card, with the price said first.
- **Professionals** close the tear. Fitters and keepers, under Mara Rowan, Jessamy Hart, and
  the harbour keeper, fill thirty-six cells on thirty-six counts.
- **The Proprietor** holds the harbour's pumps on his own pattern so that the low town does
  not flood. The operation cannot finish while he holds it, because his pattern *is* the Step.
  He lets go: of the bank, and in writing, of his foundation's claim on "all ash exhibiting the
  Step, however produced." **Master Thrale** holds a ward across the dock mouth and cannot move
  while she does.
- For some seconds two Glory fields from unlike traditions touch. **Every lamp on the coast
  shows a point of light.** Three people who have dreamed are seen to count together.

The bank comes out. The harbour's pumps stand, and the low town takes water. A professional
has died on this coast earlier in the book, and nobody dies at the Slack. The Charter Board
orders every municipal bank in the country put on its own count and kept there: a slower,
dearer, deliberately uneven grid. It is a real national answer and it costs light, money, and
an industrial house its oldest property.

**What it does not settle** is whether the Proprietor was right. He has believed for
seventy-seven years that stepped lamps are the ones that go white instead of out.

**Kiva's cost.** The hand is gone to the wrist for three weeks and returns as before. What
does not return is the rate: Aske's card now reads **one crossing in fourteen days.** Three
doors remain three clean. The fourth was asked for, by a Glory master, and she said no.

**The last of the third month.** Rhea Sorn and Kiva look again. Either may say no, and each
says no to something. Rhea names no seat; she writes Kiva's name on the Charter Board's
travelling commission, under another officer's hand, with *either of us* on it again. Kiva
takes it on two conditions of her own. Team Seven's four go four ways by their own choices.

**The last image.** Wires vouch what the ship's engineer said: ports over the water logged the
same point of light in the same minute. A hull stands in from the south with a light on its
bow that is like no lamp in Saltmere. Somebody else has dreamed, and has come to see who
answered. The disk is cold. Kiva writes that down.

## 4. Numerical and structural shape

- Six open-range movements of eight chapters; chapters 1–48; **285,000–315,000 words** unless
  scenes earn more. About 47,500–52,500 words a movement and 5,900–6,600 a chapter.
- **Viewpoint.** About 87% Kiva. About 13% across **five established lenses** at roughly 2.5 to
  3% each: **Anwen Pryce, Kellan Renn, Mara Rowan, Orla Dane, Rhea Sorn.** Cutaways are brief
  and occur where Kiva cannot witness an essential action. Perrin, Pru, Jude, Noa, Hart, and
  Dray develop through what Kiva sees. No new lens is planned.
- **Sentence and paragraph targets** (owner's formula, unchanged): mean 14.6 words; median 11;
  about 27.7% of sentences at five words or fewer; about 3.3% at forty or more; inferred
  paragraph median 18 words; scenes near 950 words, about 8.7 breaks per 10,000 words; Flesch
  Reading Ease near 72.3 and Flesch-Kincaid grade near 6.8. Measured after a movement exists.
- **Progression vocabulary** near 58 hits per 10,000 words across the book, with the opening
  third (Movements One and Two) at about 1.7 times the density of the rest.
- **Development cadence.** A Kiva realization or decision about every 2,200 words, spread
  evenly with a slight middle bump. Ten supporting people get small beats; none takes the
  secondary story.
- **Action.** Every movement has a developed physical set piece and a learning encounter.
  There is no quiet movement.

## 5. Calendar

| Span | Movement |
|---|---|
| Monday the 17th of the tenth month, 2680, to Saturday the 30th of the first month, 2681 | One |
| The second month, 2681 (Sunday the 1st to Monday the 30th), ending on the road south | Two |
| Tuesday the 1st to Sunday the 13th of the third month: Saltmere, the gate fortnight | Three |
| Monday the 14th to Wednesday the 16th: the Bench, the Carry, the Hunt | Four |
| Thursday the 17th to Sunday the 20th: the Lane, the hearing, the reading | Five |
| Monday the 21st of the third month to Thursday the 1st of the fourth: the Slack and after | Six |

The National Measure runs **Monday the 14th to Saturday the 19th of the third month, 2681.**

## 6. Six movements

### Movement One — The Second (chapters 1–8)

**Entry pressure.** Stood down from every door for eleven weeks, Kiva has to be worth
something with her hands dark. A method that worked once is being carried to seven drums and
then to the coast. A company's card says she may watch.

**Engine.** The handoff list becomes a craft. Orla tells her about Hart, and Kiva decides what
of her book a stranger may read. The new year brings the first re-reading of her figures
since Classification Day. Two nights as Pike's second teach what a professional catcher does
that he never says aloud, and where twenty-six years of it stop being enough.

**Action and turn.** Drum work under Mara. A professional call where the book answer fails
against a mixed expression and a professional is hurt. Kiva's two dead fingers are tested by
dark-hand work at a right-hand corner. The warrant for Saltmere is posted, and it is dearer
than Kellan's sum. Saltmere answers Mara's sheets: tried, and failed, on water that moves.

**Exit.** Kiva has started writing down Pike's method in seven columns. Kellan has chosen his
four. Seven's entry is signed under a captain nobody expected. After a fifteen-dram warrant,
the book has 10.53 drams left; about 5.8 of that is already spoken for by the planned
hand-to-Ember crossings and Coombe's floors, before any call reserve. The only plain
ward-trace is the sealed 0.8 medical reserve. The Measure has four boards, one of them a live
hunt.

### Movement Two — Something Quiet (chapters 9–16)

**Entry pressure.** The second month to make a four that can stand a licensed hunt against
things its kill drills were not written for: thirty days until the road south and forty-four
days from Movement One's last day to the first board, on a book whose available ash is
already spoken for.

**Engine.** Several connected chapters of training for stronger and more varied spawn under
Hunt Marshal Dinah Coombe, a Blaze who has run mixed fours on the coast: packs that keep a
count among themselves; a hem that folds like a ward; a form that takes a stacked door as an
invitation. Kiva spends her weekly go certifying a second crossing, hand to Ember, and the
hand sulks longer each time. The handed crossing is assembled from parts the reader already
owns: the plank, Pru's fold, the Line's old word, and Pike's unwritten habits.

**Action and turn.** A learning fight against Halden License in a prepared lane, where Kellan
stands opposite. Seven's first licensed hunt in its own right, in the hills round the Sag,
looking for something quiet enough to refill a medical reserve. They kill well. Nothing comes
up plain. The only plain ash on the road south is in the house of the Even Hand, whose door
has been on the latch since the summer.

**Exit.** Seven has its Flask license and a method with three weeks of rehearsal in it. Kiva
has stood near a Glory master for the first time, and the dream has come back, with a fever,
on the ferry.

### Movement Three — Water That Keeps Time (chapters 17–24)

**Entry pressure.** Saltmere is proud of its harbour bank. Thirty-six cells in step have not
dropped a stroke in six months, and the Works' reader from Meridian is a guest telling her
hosts that their best machine is a hazard.

**Engine.** Other schools solve the same problem in ways that look incompatible. The Ropewalk
School has a column for two doors; it teaches them **laid**, open together and agreeing, which
Meridian calls a stack and forbids. Dray's sealed mast rule makes the host's best candidates an
incident, and Meridian is disliked for it. Jessamy Hart turns out to be the man who can tell
the Works when the harbour is still. The harbour keeper's book has an entry for the 11th of
the third month, 2604. Something stands in the basin at each spring tide, and stands a little
taller each time. Hart and Mara seal a prediction.

**Action and turn.** The gate fortnight's seeding rounds. A coast night on Graywater's van,
called south under a Charter Board warrant. The Proprietor asks to see the plate and is
answered by a watch's vote. He then asks Kiva for the three-door, on a Halden mast, for a
price. She says no.

**Exit.** Seven is seeded in the middle of twenty-four. The prediction is under seal. Orla and
Hart have spoken once, badly.

### Movement Four — The Longshore (chapters 25–32)

**Entry pressure.** Three boards in three days, and the third is alive.

**Engine.** The Bench, without Kiva. The Carry, which is Perrin's. The Hunt, on the tidal
flats, scored on clean yield after shares, with every team's ash combed for the Step.

**Action and turn.** The Hunt is the book's midpoint set piece. Coast forms work the tide. A
laid door is entered, as Coombe's drill said it could be: Maud Lyle of the Ropewalk loses a
door, perhaps for good. A professional catcher of the Saltmere Company is killed making the
recovery that the book prescribes. The handed crossing is used for the first time against
something live, by Seven and one borrowed still-door from another circuit, and holds.

**Exit.** After three boards Seven stands ninth, a place off the roll. The Ropewalk's captain,
Juniper Carrow, has shut one of her own doors and is learning to declare. The Measure goes on
under a death.

### Movement Five — A Declared Count (chapters 33–40)

**Entry pressure.** The sealed prediction falls due on the 23rd, four days after the final.
Kiva has one crossing a week. The lane against Dray and the Slack both want it.

**Engine.** The Lane, over three days. The Charter Board's hearing on the three sheets. Draft
boards. Kellan's numbers, which run against his own house. Kiva's choice to put the dream on a
table.

**Action and turn.** Developed lane fights against rivals who have adapted: Carrow on one
door; Dray on her feet. Kiva loses the exchange she wanted, on purpose, and Seven wins the
lane. The sealed page is opened. Two Glory masters testify. The Measure is read.

**Exit.** Seven is seventh, on the roll by one mark, warranted for the Slack. Offers are on the
table for Anwen, Kellan, Jude, and Perrin. Everybody now knows that Kiva dreams.

### Movement Six — The Slack (chapters 41–48)

**Entry pressure.** A scheduled operation with a twenty-minute window, planned by people who
disagree, against a thing that has grown at every spring tide since the autumn.

**Engine.** Rehearsal, in developed scenes, with one bad assumption found in time and one that
is not. The Graving, a dry dock turned arena, becomes the purse that catches the ash when its
gate is shut.

**Action and turn.** The Slack, as section 3 gives it: three handed crossings, two failed
links, two crossings of her own, a master who has to let go, a point of light in every lamp.

**Exit.** The complete ending of section 3.

## 7. Kiva's progression ladder

| Rung | Where | What it costs or proves |
|---|---|---|
| Dark hands: a corner, a voice, a named handoff | One | Usefulness without a door. The two fingers are tested at a right-hand corner. |
| The re-reading | One | First measured figures since the spring of 2680. Draw falls a long way from 3.1 and is still poor. Ward Hold rises most. The rank frame holds **Flame** on one declared door and goes to dashes with two. No first door. Exact figures are the author's, inside those bounds. |
| A second's eye | One | She can say what a master did that he did not say. Written in seven columns. |
| Single doors relit | One, Two | Each door under Aske's eye before any crossing. |
| Hand to Ember, six times | Two | Certified by Orla on the sixth. About 1.8 drams and six of her weekly goes. |
| The handed crossing, rehearsed | Two | Three people, three doors, one caller. Works on a yard. |
| Flask license, in the four's own right | Two | A licensed hunt without a company officer's name on the entry. |
| The handed crossing, live | Four | Holds against a coast form with a borrowed still-door. |
| A crossing declined | Five | She fights Dray on one door. A loss chosen for a reason said aloud. |
| A crossing inside her certificate, in the field | Six | Ordinary terms, at last. |
| A second in the same hour | Six | The card becomes one in fourteen days. |
| The national license roll; the commission | Six | A place earned by a four and a method, not by three doors. |

**Three doors stay at three clean.** The sequence is asked for twice in this book and refused
twice. If drafting makes a fourth unavoidable, it must be bounded by a written direction, an
officer's hand, and the price said first by her healer, it must cost something that does not
come back, and it must be raised with the owner before it is written.

## 8. Team and ash ladder

- **Entry:** held 25.53; sealed 0.8; no warrant posted.
- **The warrant** is planned at three drams a head for five heads (Kiva, Anwen, Perrin, Jude,
  and Pru as reserve): 15.0. Kellan's place is paid by the Halden license four.
- **Planned spends before Saltmere:** six hand-to-Ember crossings, about 1.8; Coombe's floors,
  about 4.0; a call reserve; one crossing a week kept for Ember to still when the hand allows.
  The book runs short in the second month, on purpose, and the licensed hunt has to pay.
- **Plain ash is the scarce thing.** Every natural yield now carries the Step in trace. Taking
  a bank out of step needs a jar of plain ash a cell. Seven's eight-tenths of sealed plain
  ward-trace becomes worth more than the rest of its book, and an honest man offers to buy it.
- **Exit:** the author reconciles every dram. No free practice fuel. The red bags stay
  evidence.

## 9. Relationship arcs

| Pair | From | To |
|---|---|---|
| Kiva and **Pike** | Second and master; mouth shut | He is hurt and becomes a voice at her hem. Each has been the other's second. |
| Kiva and **Rhea Sorn** | A bounded card | Rhea takes a count from her catcher at the Slack and gives her name to another officer. |
| Kiva and **Orla** | Sponsor's line | Orla asks the Chair to strike her own line. Kiva's certificate stands in Kiva's name. |
| Kiva and **Mara** | Guardian who will not own the choice | Kiva has to watch Mara take the national risk, and not own that either. The seal is broken by both. |
| Kiva and **Anwen** | Reconciled, careful | Equals who are about to work in different places. Anwen takes a seat of her own. |
| Kiva and **Perrin** | Envy said aloud | He captains, wins a board, and finds that being envied is worse. |
| Kiva and **Kellan** | A blade told where he is wanted | He stands for Halden License, fights her, and then puts his numbers where they hurt his house. |
| Kiva and **Pru** | Catcher and cord | Pru catches a national board in her place and is not grateful, only good. |
| Kiva and **Selka Dray** | Unpersuaded | Dray wins her lane, calls the count again on her feet, and is still not a friend. |
| Kiva and **Jessamy Hart** | A card she has not seen | Two people with a bad hand and a book between them. He teaches her what it is for. |
| **Orla** and **Hart** | Twenty years of not writing | One true sentence each. No absolution. |
| Kiva and **Juniper Carrow** | The nation's best, on a doctrine Meridian forbids | Each uses a piece of the other's method at the Slack. |

## 10. Action, training, and learning promises

1. **Dark-hand work under load** (One): a drum that will not come out; a corner at a hem.
2. **A professional call that exceeds the book** (One), developed, with the correction shown.
3. **Connected spawn-training chapters** (Two): at least three new behaviors, each met first
   as a failed attempt, then as a drill, then under pressure.
4. **A learning fight against Halden License** (Two) that leaves a correction Seven can try
   again.
5. **A licensed hunt in the hills** (Two): terrain, yield, grading, and a disappointment.
6. **Seeding rounds against an unlike doctrine** (Three): the lay beats Seven fairly.
7. **The Hunt on the Longshore** (Four): the long set piece of the middle. A death.
8. **The Carry** (Four): Perrin's board, a full scene.
9. **The Lane against Carrow, and against Dray** (Five): two long fights, each changing
   problem by problem.
10. **The Slack** (Six): rehearsal, operation, and aftermath.

Fights follow the seat's rule: terrain, starting positions, reach, objectives, injuries, and
limits are set privately first; the opponent learns too; costs carry forward.

## 11. Evidence ladder for the filter and the dream

| Movement | What accumulates | What the public calls it |
|---|---|---|
| One | No plain ward-trace in Meridian. A mixed expression on a night call. Saltmere's failure. | A gifted class; a bad winter. |
| Two | Nothing quiet in the hills. The Order's cellar. Kiva's dream returns near a Glory master. | A bad rift season. |
| Three | A second keeper's book for the 2604 night. The basin at each spring tide. The notch is shorter on the coast than at home. | A coast problem. |
| Four | A laid door entered. Coast forms on the tide. | The worst Hunt in memory. |
| Five | The sealed page. Two living masters. Returns from remarks columns in other cities. Old Halden bench books that give the Step a slower count than today's. | **The Unroofed Sky.** |
| Six | A coast-wide point of light. Wires from over the water. | A national fact, with no agreed meaning. |

What the book establishes by its end: the Step is the mark that the rising pressure leaves in
ash; prepared people and rifts are answering the same pressure; it is quickening; it is not
only this country. What it does not establish: what the pressure is, why it keeps a count,
what the gathering is for, or what happens when the roof is off.

## 12. Plant and payoff ledger

| Plant (where) | Payoff (planned) |
|---|---|
| "The count of a thing that keeps time (somebody else's)" (Book One) | Dray calls it again. The handed crossing is built on a count that is not the caller's. |
| The plank; Pru's fold; "a crossing," 2471 (Book One) | The handed crossing (Two, Four, Six). |
| "It stays eight-tenths until somebody kills something quiet" (Book One) | Nothing is quiet any more (Two). Plain ash is the price of the Slack (Six). |
| The method "needs the main to be still" (Book One) | Tidal water (One, Three). The slack (Six). |
| Mara's sealed page (Book One) | Opened before the Charter Board (Five). |
| Lumb: "somebody at Saltmere had a temperature as well" (Book One) | The harbour keeper's book (Three). |
| The 2611 settlement over Annick's box (Book One) | The Proprietor went looking for her account after 2604 (Five). |
| "The Proprietor claims the Step, and all ash exhibiting the Step" (Book One) | He lets the claim go (Six). |
| Hart: "I knew it with the hand, the bad one" (Book One) | What a ruined hand can still be asked (One, Three). |
| Dray: "I mean to beat you in a lane there" (Book One) | She does (Five). |
| Rhea: "either of us may say no" (Book One) | Both do (Six). |
| "If T7 is called on a night of mine, she is T7's and I go short" (Book One) | Rhea goes short on a coast night that costs her (Four). |
| An unvouched overseas line (Book One) | Vouched by wire; a hull (Six). |
| A can cut with soot in the Works' store (Book One) | Somebody has been stretching civic stock; it surfaces in the count of plain ash (Two or Three). Small, human, not a conspiracy. |
| Pike's unwritten habits (One) | Seven columns that a Saltmere catcher reads (Four, Six). |
| The warrant and the captain (One) | Perrin's board (Four). |
| Coombe: a laid door is an open door (Two) | Maud Lyle (Four). Carrow's adaptation (Five, Six). |
| The basin at each spring tide; a sealed prediction (Three) | The Slack (Six). |
| One crossing in seven days (Book One, carried) | The lane given up (Five). Two in an hour (Six). One in fourteen. |
| The Order's house on the latch (Book One) | Entered on Kiva's terms, for ash and not for a year (Two). |
| Jude: "I'd want to see the sea" (Book One) | He stays beside it (Six). |

## 13. Reserved (do not spend in Book Two)

The full loss of the filter. Pure Glory presence. The cause of the Fall. The disk's origin and
purpose, and the first bearer's book. Why the pressure keeps a count. Who or what is "paying
attention." The color with no word. Whether Hesper Annick, who walked out in 2461, died. Any
returning figure from the first Kindled trilogy. A doctrine that makes the schools obsolete.
The resolution of anything international. The registry word "Breach," which stays unused
unless the owner asks for it.

## 14. Movement packet rule

Each movement gets one compact brief: entry state, pressure, a few consequences, boundaries,
output paths, and room. `packets/MOVEMENT-001.md` is written. Later packets are written from
the surviving prose of the movement before, never from this map alone.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-national-measure/CHARACTERS.md

# Kindling 2680, Book Two — cast

> Built by TAC

Status: **planning.** Existing names, roles, pronouns, bodies, and facts are locked by Book
One and its checkpoints. Book Two wants and turns are `PLANNED`. New names are the planner's,
checked by search against every manuscript in this repository and `universe/`, and are
**pending owner review** like the minor names Book One introduced. A role in square brackets
has no name yet; the author names it at need, after a registry check, and records it.

## Continuing cast

| Character | Locked function | Book Two want | Planned turn |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Kiva Rowan** | Seventeen; three-door catcher; seated, unclassified; bearer of the plate | A professional future on ordinary terms: to be useful for something another person could also learn | Stops being the only hand that can do the thing. Builds, writes down, and calls a method for three people. Puts the dream on a table by her own choice. |
| **Orla Dane** | Accountable sponsor and teacher; Torch; twenty years on the Line; bad knee | To do right by Hart without using Kiva to do it | Tells Kiva about the card herself, early. Meets Hart and is not forgiven. Asks the Chair to strike her own line once it is no longer needed. |
| **Mara Rowan** | Aunt, guardian; Grade Two; reader of the trunk; one reading hand | To have her method read properly, and to keep the hand she has left | Becomes the person the country sends for. Teaches thirty-six readers and does not read the bank herself. Breaks her own seal. |
| **Anwen Pryce** | Hold; has the wax; a summer place on Mace's ward line | A field placement that is hers, and to be the best student wall in the country | Leads a mixed ward line at the Slack. Takes a seat of her own and tells Kiva first, plainly. |
| **Perrin Cade** | Carry; calls *off*; ribs sprung; his Hall's second team | To have something with only his name on it | Made captain for the entry, to his horror. Wins the Carry. Learns that being envied is no better. |
| **Kellan Renn** | Fire; blade; the Halden yard; on Seven's book | For the number to keep meaning what it says | Stands for Halden License. Sees what his house wants from the Measure. Reads out figures that cost it. |
| **Jude Talley** | Kill by name; 7.8 drams in an autumn | To be drafted on his own line, and to see the sea | Drafted by the Saltmere Company. Stays on the coast. |
| **Pru Finch** | Catcher with her own sheet; the cord; reserve | Not to be anybody's cord | Second on the night van beside Kiva. Catches a national board when Kiva is down with fever. |
| **Noa Bexley** | Reader to the Chair's office; three columns | Credit, and to be right slowly | Carries the mast to Saltmere under Dray's rule. Runs the reading of remarks columns with Lumb's notion. Adds a fourth column and will not draw a line between them. |
| **Ysra Crest** | Placement Chair; ward-holder on the 2660 Bench | A process that survives a case it did not foresee | Sits at the national table. Meets a school with a column for two doors and keeps her page instead. Writes its third line. |
| **Rhea Sorn** | Graywater officer; Torch; six columns on her slate | To find out whether she can train someone without owning her | Takes her van south. Goes short on a night that costs her. Takes a count from her own second. Gives Kiva's name away. |
| **Pike** | Graywater's catcher, twenty-six years | To hand on what he knows before his hands decide for him | Teaches by being watched. Is hurt on a night call. Kneels at Kiva's hem as a voice. |
| **Hollis Vane** | Home Line foreman | Capable people on real ground, and the reasons written | Calls Kiva off the roll for the first time, at a day rate. Directs the Line's part at the Slack. |
| **Selka Dray** | Blade, Team Twenty-Eight; the mast rule; unpersuaded | To win on a floor where everything is declared | Walks. Beats Kiva in a lane. Calls the count at the Slack on her feet. Does not become a friend. |
| **Lowen Brask** | Old Four; reads spawn forms | A Line seat | Names the coast forms first. Two to the bar again, with a stranger. |
| **Healer Aske** | Keeps Kiva's card | Not to be talked into a smaller number | Travels with the Circuit's first company. Says the price twice. Writes the fourteen. |
| **Jessamy Hart** | Orla's candidate of 2659; twin-kindled, Ember and hand; hurt at the Bench in 2660; left at nineteen; pump-fitter at Saltmere; about forty | To be read as a fitter, and to read what the girl wrote | Tells the Works when the harbour is still. Shows Kiva what a ruined hand can be asked. Keeps his doors shut by choice. |
| **Mr. Adlam** | Halden technical office; honest; often astonished | For the patent to be defended truthfully or not at all | Brings the old bench books. Offers a fair price for Seven's plain ash and takes no for an answer. |
| **Officer Yeo** | Saltmere Company officer | A harbour that does not depend on one engine | Host and ally. Loses a catcher. |
| **Doran Mace, Nairn, Ruddock, Tibb, Stiles** | Graywater | — | The van goes south with them. |
| **Examiner Rusk, Lumb, A. Renn, Halloran, the Reader** | Works, pump house, grading, shelf, Library | — | Rusk and A. Renn travel. Lumb's notion becomes a national reading. Halloran keeps her shelf. |
| **Wilf Marten, Wace, Hendry Moss, Gower, Edda, Hesketh, Yarrow, Pettit, Foreman Birch** | The other Meridian fours | — | They go to Saltmere. Some stand in the cohort at the Slack. |

## New for Book Two (names pending owner review)

| Name or role | Function | Independent want | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Dinah Coombe** (she) | The Charter Board's Hunt Marshal; **Blaze**; ran mixed fours on the coast for thirty years | A national roll of fours that come home | Prepares each circuit's first company for the Hunt. Teaches in drills, not speeches. The book's visible standard above Torch. |
| **Juniper Carrow** (she, eighteen) | Captain and laid blade of the Ropewalk School's first four; the national favourite | A Saltmere Company contract that keeps her on her own harbour | Beats Seven fairly. Adapts after the Hunt by shutting a door. Not a villain and not a convert. |
| **Maud Lyle** (she) | Carrow's laid second | To be as good as Carrow says she is | Her lay is entered at the Hunt. The cost is a door. |
| **Ambrose Halden**, "the Proprietor" (he) | Present holder of the Halden foundation and its pattern; **Glory**; alive in 2604 | To have the country ready when the roof comes off, as he understands ready | Industrial titan with a defensible reading of the dream. Asks Kiva for the three-door. Lets go of the Step. Never certain he was wrong. |
| **Clemency Thrale** (she) | Master of the Order of the Even Hand; **Glory**; one door for life | To keep one quiet thing in the world quiet | Holds the last plain ash on the road. Testifies. Holds a ward and cannot move. |
| **Keeper Peverell** (she) | Keeper of the Saltmere harbour pump house | Not to be the keeper who stopped the pumps | Her house's book has the 2604 night. Fills the bank with Mara and Hart. |
| [CLERK OF THE MEASURE] | The Charter Board's official who runs the National Measure and hears the three sheets | An event that is read to the end | Coins a phrase for the papers. |
| [COAST CATCHER] | A Saltmere Company catcher | — | Dies at the Hunt making the recovery the book prescribes. Named when written; the name must carry weight in later chapters. |
| [SHIP'S ENGINEER] | The man off a hull from over the water | To be believed | Unvouched in Book One; vouched by wire in Movement Six. |
| [THE ONE ON THE HULL] | A Glory-level stranger, seen as a light | Unknown | The last image only. Not met, named, or explained in Book Two. |
| [FOUR OTHER CIRCUITS] | Sixteen more fours | — | Named sparingly, as the lanes need them. One lends a still-door to Seven at the Hunt. |

## Places and bodies new to Book Two (planned names)

- **The Ropewalk School**, Saltmere: the host circuit's leading school. Teaches two doors
  **laid** together. Its circuit has a column for it.
- **The Graving:** Saltmere's national floor, a dry dock turned arena, with a gate that keeps
  the harbour out.
- **The Longshore:** the tidal flats where the Hunt is held.
- **The Saltmere Company** (green; founded before 2362) and **Graywater** (slate blue) are
  Book One's. **The Charter Board** is Book One's.

## Viewpoint

About 87% Kiva. Five established lenses share about 13%: **Anwen, Kellan, Mara, Orla, Rhea.**
Each cutaway shows an action Kiva cannot see. Perrin, who had a lens in Book One, is seen
through Kiva in Book Two; his board is hers to watch.

## Development budget

The lead has a steady realization or decision about every 2,200 words. Small, independent
beats are spread across **Anwen, Perrin, Kellan, Pru, Jude, Noa, Orla, Mara, Rhea, and Pike,**
with Dray, Hart, and Carrow as the rivals and mirror who adapt. No single friend or mentor
takes the secondary story.

## Voices to keep

Anwen owns "I want it said" and "What's behind it?" Perrin minds at length and is funny about
it. Kellan does sums aloud and says "I'd expect nothing else." Pru says a big thing and goes
scarlet. Jude scratches his jaw and asks what ash is for. Mara is dry and exact. Orla is rough
and brief. Rhea allows herself one piece of advice a season. Pike nearly smiles. Dray's face
is a shut door. Aske counts so that others need not.

## Name and narration checks

- Avoid aural collisions with Book One's cast and with Callie, Jab, Toren, Rook, Elias, Vera,
  Dessa, Milo, Wyck, Senna, Dee, Katori, Tamar, and Meridian. "Keld" is taken (Idony Keld).
- No name or near-homophone from the research corpus.
- Checked and unused at planning: Coombe, Dinah, Carrow, Juniper, Lyle, Maud, Ambrose,
  Clemency, Thrale, Peverell, Ropewalk, Graving, Longshore.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-national-measure/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md

# Kindling 2680, Book Two — universe bible

> Built by TAC

Status: **planning.** This file does not replace
`books/book-2680-opening/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md` or `universe/UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md`; read both. It
restates the rules Book Two leans on and adds only what Book Two needs. Three labels are used
and kept apart:

- `ESTABLISHED` — on the page of Book One or locked in its bible.
- `PLANNED` — intended for Book Two. Not canon until surviving prose says it.
- `RESERVED` — true or possible in the series, and not to be spent in Book Two.

## 1. Rules that do not move (`ESTABLISHED`)

- One source, three traditions: Ember, rift-granted magic (the hand door: Ward, Sustain), and
  internal cultivation (the still door). The soul fills. Ash is ownerless, finite fuel.
- The rank ladder: Kindled, Ember, Flame, Fire, Torch, Blaze, Glory. Rank and breadth are one
  axis; skill and control are another. Hold, Draw, Call, yield, grade, resonance, license,
  and standing are separate measures, and none pays for another.
- **Draw** is ash spent on a piece of work over the book expectation for it: 1.0 is standard,
  lower is better.
- A kill does not raise rank. Ash funds the next attempt. Learning is what makes it survivable.
- Ash is recorded by **yield** (Handful, Flask, Barrel, Well), **grade**, and **resonance.**
  The comb's third reed sings for ward-trace (W). The seventh (I) is cut to the Step.
- Glory stops ordinary aging, is rare, and cannot be granted, bought, or stolen. It widens
  what one person can hold: throughput, duration, precision. It never makes ash free.
- Riftspawn expressions echo worked powers with nobody home to direct them. Spawn anatomy
  as trained: breath; posts or load paths; knots; a cord; a hem; the tear; closure.
- Students close no tear. Licensed crews do. Radiation work and licenses not held are not
  taken over in a crisis.
- Classification is physical: rigs, masts, pens, tapes, projected tables, human rulings. No
  private overlay.
- **Doors, for Kiva.** A competition is all doors open and fighting. A stack is two open
  together and agreeing: soft, cheap, and open to anything near; forbidden to her. A sequence
  shuts each door before the next opens, and each gap costs about a quarter of a dram with
  nothing open. A crossing is one gap. Three doors are two gaps.
- **The disk** does not teach, speak, map, choose, open a door, or answer. Two bearers have
  written *warm, once,* after a first crossing, and *cold* ever after.
- **The dream** is a fever, not a prophecy that can be consulted. It cannot be summoned. Each
  recurrence has a bodily cost. Triggers on record: fever, ash overload, filter instability,
  close contact with Glory.
- **The Step** (Halden's foundation patent, 2391): a row of cells salted late with one pinch
  of ash already made to keep a beat thereafter beats as one and can be driven by one
  operator. Used in Halden's yards, refineries, lift yards, and big tram sheds. Pump houses
  and tenement boxes were never meant to be stepped. The claim covers "all ash exhibiting the
  Step, however produced." The caution: "Not to be introduced to an unstepped bank under
  load." Before the patent the Wren House called it *counting ash.*
- Shown, not explained, in Book One: stepped pumps make water rise and fall on a count of four
  and four; a cell joined to a stepped bank by nothing but that water goes into step; a tear
  wants "the thing that keeps time" and not the bright thing; a string with an arm in the
  water is itself a pump; cut cords spill open ash that can cook a bank.

## 2. The country (`PLANNED`, built on `ESTABLISHED` pieces)

- **The Charter Board** (`ESTABLISHED` as a name) licenses rift companies nationally, keeps the
  national roll, and runs the National Measure. It can ask a nation to look at its banks. A
  Circuit cannot.
- **Six circuits** send their first company of four fours each: **twenty-four fours.** Meridian
  sends Halden License, Old Four, Team Twenty-Eight, and Team Seven. Saltmere hosts. The other
  four circuits are named as the lanes need them.
- **The National Measure,** Monday the 14th to Saturday the 19th of the third month, 2681.
  **Four boards,** as at the Regional, each marked by place, twenty-four down to one, and
  added. The Hunt stands where the Regional's Call stood:
  1. **The Bench** — banks of cells with a fault planted; find, quench, re-tune, restart.
  2. **The Carry** — rescue and extraction against a clock.
  3. **The Hunt** — a licensed live hunt on tidal ground under company officers, marked on
     clean yield after shares, less every remark. Students close nothing.
  4. **The Lane** — the declared-role lane, as at home: three for the litter over a team's own
     line, one for a down, two halves a down. Run as a bracket over three days.
- **The national license roll.** The first eight fours are licensed for the year to stand
  Barrel-class responses under a company officer. Placement therefore decides who is sent for.
- **Gate rules.** Every dram is combed. Dray's rule arrives under seal: any candidate with
  more than one door is read from a sanctioned mast by a reader of the Meridian office. The
  host's laid candidates fall under it.
- **The warrant.** A circuit's fours travel on their own books. Planned at three drams a head.
- **Draft boards** sit through the Lane days. A company may card a student; a student may say
  no. A card is not a seat.
- **Travel.** Two hundred miles: the road round the Sag, a ferry, a river, and the coast boat.
  Station transit exists and is too dear for a student four.

## 3. Saltmere (`PLANNED`)

- A harbour city. The Saltmere Company's green is `ESTABLISHED`, as is its harbour bank of
  thirty-six cells, in step since twenty past two on the 17th of the ninth month, 2680.
- **The Ropewalk School** leads the host circuit. It teaches two-door candidates to open both
  doors together and twist them to agree: **a lay,** *laid* doors. It is what Meridian calls a
  stack. It is cheap, quick, and soft, it wins lanes, and its circuit has a column for it. It
  is not foolish. It was built against people and against the spawn of forty years ago.
- **The Graving:** a dry dock roofed with tiers. A floating gate keeps the harbour out. With
  the gate shut and the sluices closed, the dock is a basin of still water whatever the tide
  does outside.
- **The Longshore:** miles of tidal flat and creek where the coast's tears open.
- **The harbour.** Pumps keep the low town dry. The harbour's water moves twice a day and is
  still only at slack, for about twenty minutes. The Ferren method needs still water. Mara's
  sheets were tried at Saltmere in the winter and failed for that reason.
- **Many in Saltmere like the stepped bank.** It has not dropped a stroke. One operator can
  drive it. That is a fair position held by sensible people.

## 4. Spawn in a national season (`PLANNED`)

All new behavior obeys trained anatomy and combines or redirects it. None is a bigger Flask.
Working descriptions follow; the crews on the page give the names, as Book One's did.

- **Packs.** Husks that keep a count among themselves and move on it. Cutting one out of time
  breaks the pack for a few counts. Cutting in time does nothing.
- **A folding hem.** A hem that folds from its far edge, as a catcher's ward does, and so
  comes off a purse sheet.
- **An entering form.** A cord that goes to an open stack as a string goes to water that
  keeps time. A sequenced door gives it nothing to enter. A laid door is an open door.
- **Coast forms.** Things that stand in tidal creeks and work the flood and the ebb, where no
  sheet can be laid and ash goes into the water.
- **The first Barrel.** The expression in the harbour basin, grown at each spring tide. Its
  shape is the author's. It is not the Pit's square and not Stopgate's string abreast.
- **Yield** is higher on average and nearly all of it reads I above trace. Rich ash that
  cannot go on a floor, into a pump house, or into a medical tin.

## 5. Ash in a national season (`PLANNED`)

- **Plain ash is disappearing.** Natural yield carried the Step in trace by the spring of 2680
  (`ESTABLISHED`: "it's all had the hair since the thaw"). By the winter no fresh kill reads
  plain, in Meridian or in the hills.
- **Plain ash is what un-stepping needs:** a jar a cell, of ash that "has never heard a beat."
  Old stock is finite: reference shelves, wardens' winter stores, sealed medical reserves, and
  the cellars of an Order that never stepped anything.
- So the price of ash turns over. The richest red bags are close to worthless for safe work,
  and eight-tenths of a dram of plain ward-trace is worth an argument.
- A healer can ease a **starved** hand with plain ward-trace and cannot ease one that is
  **turned out** (`ESTABLISHED`). Stepped ward-trace is no use for it.

## 6. The handed crossing (`PLANNED`; the working term may be replaced on the page)

- **What it is.** Catch, carry, and plant, each done by a different person through one door,
  with a caller. A hand door lifts and folds. An Ember door takes the folded load across. A
  still door plants it. Between people there is no quarter-dram gap, because nobody shuts and
  opens.
- **What it costs instead.** For an instant at each pass the load belongs to nobody. A pass
  called half a count wrong drops it or nips someone (`ESTABLISHED`: Pru's fingers). Each
  holder must take a count that is not theirs. It needs rehearsal with those exact people.
- **Why it was not already done.** Schools teach one door and declared roles; a pass between
  unlike doors belongs to nobody's curriculum. The Home Line had a word for it once. A
  single-door fighter cannot feel the far side of a gap. Somebody who has been through both
  can call it.
- **Limits.** It is slower to set than a three-door sequence. It fails if any one of three
  people is hurt, late, or unwilling. It cannot be done by strangers. It does not close a
  tear, kill anything, or mend a bank.
- **Kiva's part** is the call, the ground test, and the written columns. Under her certificate
  she may also stand as one link by making one declared crossing on a sanctioned mast.

## 7. What Book Two establishes about the Step, the filter, and the dream (`PLANNED`)

Each line is to be earned by evidence on the page and said by somebody who can be wrong.

1. **The Step is a mark.** It is what rising pressure from beyond the filter leaves in ash.
   Halden did not invent the beat in 2391; the foundation found counting ash, learned to
   induce it, and built an industry on it.
2. **Tears come to it.** A stepped bank is a worked pattern keeping time at scale, and spawn
   expressions echo worked patterns. That is why a hold stood, and why a string went to the
   drain.
3. **People and spawn are answering one pressure.** Noa's columns get a fourth, for ash, and
   it rises with the other three.
4. **It is quickening.** Old Halden bench books give the Step a slower count than today's
   four and four. (The figures are the author's, after reading the patent scene in Book One.)
5. **It is not uniform.** The notch that follows Kiva's cliff by eleven seconds at Meridian
   follows it sooner on the coast. Nobody can say what that distance is a distance to.
6. **The dream is shared.** Annick (Glory, 2431; her entry undated); the Ferren keeper and the
   Saltmere keeper on one night in 2604; Kiva in 2676, proved independent by a sealed and timed
   page; Ambrose Halden in 2604 and since; Clemency Thrale; and returns from other cities'
   remarks columns.
7. **A T-0 can be local and can be wide.** When two Glory fields from unlike traditions touch
   at the Slack, every lamp on the coast shows a point of light, and ports overseas log the
   same minute.
8. **The readings disagree.** Halden: step the country, so that its lamps go white and not
   out. The Even Hand: one door, one quiet thing, and let nothing in that asks. The Works and
   the Line: whatever is coming, do not be one engine tonight. Book Two acts on the third and
   does not disprove the first.

## 8. Reserved truths (`RESERVED`)

- The complete loss of the filter, and Glory presence, belong to Book Three.
- What the pressure is; why it keeps a count; what is "paying attention."
- The cause of the Fall. The Makers.
- The disk's origin and use, the nine rings, and the first bearer's book. In Book Two the disk
  is cold, including beside two Glory masters.
- The color with no word. Only Kiva and Annick's page have it.
- Whether Hesper Annick died in the winter rifts of 2461.
- What the 2446 and 2604 lamp nights were *caused* by.
- Kiva's classification. No column is made for her in Book Two.
- Whether three doors can be made safe or taught. They are not done in Book Two.
- Any returning figure from the first trilogy.
- A single doctrine. The lay keeps its uses. The Order keeps its door. The handed crossing is
  one method.
- Anything settled between nations.

## 9. Guardrails for the author

- Every climax capability is shown and priced earlier.
- A Glory master on the page has a limit the reader can see: a place they cannot leave, a
  time they cannot exceed, a thing they must hold or let go. No Glory master does a student's
  or a fitter's work.
- No fingers heal. No rank, ash, device, or result restores them.
- No institution is a villain. The Halden Works and the Municipal Works stay distinct.
- Ledgers reconcile to the hundredth.
- Dream knowledge moves only by a decision made on the page.
- Nothing from the research corpus: no names, terms, scenes, or lines.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-national-measure/SERIES_MAP.md

# Kindling 2680 — series map, as of Book Two planning

> Built by TAC

Status: **Book One complete and accepted (2026-10-12). Book Two planned, not drafted. Book
Three is horizon.** This file carries the series forward from
`books/book-2680-opening/SERIES_MAP.md`. It does not rewrite Book One; where they differ on
anything Book One put on the page, Book One's manuscript and ledger govern.

## Series promise (unchanged)

A generation born into mature door schools finds that its institutions kept the founders'
methods and simplified away the reasons. A three-door catcher has to rebuild connection
between divided traditions before a failing filter makes that division fatal. The ladder runs
from collegiate competition into the professional rift companies that defend and power the
rebuilt world.

## Repeating pleasures (unchanged, with Book Two's emphasis)

- Visible practice, measurable correction, rematches, standings, recruitment, and earned
  change of place.
- Schools that solve one obstacle in ways that look incompatible. **Book Two adds a school
  that teaches the forbidden thing well.**
- The earned-ash loop: train, fight, kill, contain, recover, grade, divide, invest. **Book Two
  turns it over once: the scarce ash is the plain kind.**
- Rift set pieces where teamwork and ground matter more than output.
- Records that change a present choice.
- Friends, rivals, and mentors with stakes of their own.
- The Unroofed Sky, as fallible human testimony and never as instruction.
- *Why the post is crooked.* **Book Two's companion question: what is being measured, and
  who keeps the count?**

## NOW — what Book One left true

| Thread | Carried state |
|---|---|
| Kiva | Seventeen. Seated, Third Division, catcher; unclassified. On the Home Line's roll. One certified crossing. Three clean three-door sequences, "not to be asked of her." Two fingers permanently without fine sensation. Nothing lit until the new year. |
| Team Seven | Fourth at the Regional; second step in its own right; held 25.53, sealed 0.8. Goes to the National Measure with Halden License, Old Four, and Team Twenty-Eight. |
| Professional world | Graywater's bounded card: catcher's second, six nights at most, no seat promised. |
| The grid | One bank taken out of step by a written method that needs still water. Seven drums waiting. Saltmere's bank of thirty-six still one engine. |
| The rifts | Holds stood by husks; a string paid out by a drain; stepped ash; skins in numbers. |
| The record | The plate recognized and silent. Two unlike people, lifetimes apart, wrote that there was no roof. Three sheets before the Charter Board. An unvouched line from overseas. |
| People | Mara with one reading hand on a card. Orla with a card from the south coast that Kiva has not seen. Dray on her feet by the third month. |

The full entry state is `STATE_LEDGER.md`.

## NEXT — Book Two, *The National Measure*

**Scale:** national. Saltmere, six circuits, twenty-four fours, licensed hunts, draft boards,
an industrial Glory house, and a school order, with the Charter Board over all of them.

**Promise.** Kiva earns a place among the country's best on a method other people can use.
The competition is read to its end. A scheduled national operation takes a harbour bank out
of step on tidal water, and the country chooses a slower, uneven grid.

**Irreversible changes by its end:**

1. The Unroofed Sky is named in public, attested by a sealed page, four records, and two
   living Glory masters, who read it in opposite ways.
2. The Step is understood as the mark that rising pressure leaves in ash, and as the thing
   that tears come to. Human and spawn escalation are shown to be answers to one pressure.
   The filter trend is established as a trend. Its cause is not.
3. The Halden foundation gives up its claim on the Step. Municipal banks go onto their own
   counts by order.
4. A mixed cohort exists: students of four circuits and three companies who have done one
   hard thing together by a written method. It is the seed of a national team.
5. Kiva holds two certified crossings and a slower card: one in fourteen days. She is on the
   national license roll with her four, and under a Charter Board commission to carry the
   method.
6. Team Seven's four choose four different futures.
7. Ports over the water saw the same light in the same minute, and somebody has sailed
   toward it.

**The coupled ladder in Book Two.**

| Side | Regional (Book One) | National (Book Two) |
|---|---|---|
| People | A strong class; one-door discipline; Kiva begins to sequence | National fours; laid doors as a taught doctrine; a Blaze teaching mixed fours; the handed crossing; two Glory masters at work |
| Spawn | More husks and Flasks; skins; a hold; a paid-out string | Packs that keep a count; hems that fold; forms that enter an open stack; coast forms on the tide; the first Barrel |
| Ash | Small shares; one rich, red-tagged yield | Higher average yield, nearly all of it stepped; **plain ash near extinction**; the price of a medical reserve |

## HORIZON — Book Three (not planned in detail)

**Scale:** international. National teams and companies, cross-border grids, rival doctrines,
Glory masters, apex rifts.

Fixed by the owner: the planetary filter is completely lost; pure Glory presence becomes the
world's permanent condition; the survivors begin a shared next-ladder method, with no single
ruler. The dream does not fix who gathers, who cooperates, what survives, or who leads.

Book Two hands Book Three these live questions and answers none of them:

- Was the Proprietor right that stepped lamps go white and plain ones go out? The country has
  just bet against him.
- Who was on the hull, and what did they see in their own fever?
- Can a handed crossing be called across languages and doctrines that share no count?
- What does a card of one crossing in fourteen days allow, when the pressure doubles?
- Three doors, three clean, a fourth unspent.
- The plate: cold beside two Glory masters. The first book: still lost.
- Hesper Annick walked out in 2461 and the Order wrote her down as dead.

## Deep mystery budget

Book One identified the plate's bearer line. **Book Two may establish the filter trend, name
the Step as its mark, and make the dream a public, shared, and disputed fact.** It may not
remove the filter, deliver Glory presence, explain the Fall, give the disk an origin or use,
identify the Makers, or crown one doctrine. Book Three may cross the threshold while leaving
ultimate cosmology open.

## Series checks at this turn

- **Power inflation.** Kiva ends Book Two with no new door, no fourth sequence, and a slower
  card. Her gain is a second certified crossing and a method that makes other people stronger.
- **Repeated plot.** Book One struck its tournament and then met a surprise at home. Book Two
  reads its tournament to the end and then runs a *scheduled* operation, in daylight, that
  everybody has argued about for three weeks.
- **Cross-series consequence.** The first trilogy's records still govern. Nothing in this
  plan needs a founder to have been wrong.

## Connection to the founders (unchanged)

Toren and Rook: mixed-door field teaching. Wyck: body and safe failure. Dee, Jab, and Homura:
Sustain, Ward, ash depth, rotation, abort lines. Callie: boundaries and making a safe place
for another's work. Dessa and Senna: append-only evidence and the reasons column. The Home
Line: the field arm, never merely a team. Tamar Vey: a teacher who carried one question from
workshop to workshop. Book Two's last page sends a catcher out on the same errand.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-national-measure/planning/SONNET-5.5-MOVEMENT-001-SHOWRUNNER.md

# Sonnet 5.5 showrunner pass: Movement One packet

> Built by TAC

- **Runtime model:** Sonnet 5.5, model identifier `claude-sonnet-5-5` (as reported by the
  session; not independently verifiable from inside it).
- **Date and branch:** 2026-10-12, `codex/kindling-2680-book-02`, working directory
  `/Users/drive/kindling-2680-book-02`.
- **Scope kept:** one packet edited (`packets/MOVEMENT-001.md`), this report written. No
  manuscript prose, no chapter cards, no commit, push, publication, narration, cold read, or
  Opus session. Nothing in the packet's owner-locked outcomes, the 48-chapter architecture,
  Book One canon, or the book's ending was altered.

## 1. Files read (all in full)

Book Two: `OWNER_BRIEF.md`, `BOOK_MAP.md`, `STATE_LEDGER.md`, `CHARACTERS.md`,
`UNIVERSE_BIBLE.md`, `SERIES_MAP.md`, `packets/MOVEMENT-001.md` (before and after editing).
Book One: `provenance/MOVEMENT-006-CONTINUITY.md`, `manuscript/chapter-41.md` through
`chapter-48.md`, in order. Pen-name seat: `oconnor/PIPELINE.md`, `oconnor/VOICE.md`,
`oconnor/FORMULA.md`, and `research/ironprince-craft-formula.md`. The research file was read
as a rhythm and cadence source only; nothing from it is a source of prose, names, scenes, or
protected expression.

Two targeted checks beyond the list, to settle suspected conflicts: a grep of chapters 45 to
48 for the coast-boat date (chapter 48, line 295), and a grep for Kiva's Bench status
(chapter 36, line 319; chapters 44 and 46 on who "stood a Bench").

## 2. What was already strong (preserved)

- **The brief form.** Entry state, pressure, a short list of fixed turns, protected
  boundaries, and an explicit list of what the author owns. It is not eight chapter
  contracts, and I added none.
- **The engine sentence.** "The dark weeks should teach the thing the van nights test. The
  second night should change what the four must train for. The warrant should make that
  training a matter of ash." That is a real causal chain and the right size for a movement.
- **The central question.** "What is she for, dark?" answered small and true, with dramatic
  irony the packet tells Opus to keep from Kiva ("She should not know that yet").
- **Honest cost carried forward.** Perrin's ribs, Mara's dated hand, the 0.8 reserve, the
  dark rule, the fingers that do not improve, the cold disk, the dream that does not recur.
- **The second van night as a rule-edge dilemma.** Kiva sees from her knees and is bound by
  "mouth shut until the bag is sealed." That is a better scene question than any fight
  choreography, and it was left to the author.
- **Lessons from Book One's measurement.** The packet already says narration, not dialogue,
  should carry the long sentences. I left the formula section intact.
- **Boundaries.** Hart is a card; Saltmere is reached only by letter or wire; Coombe is
  announced and not met; nobody explains the Step; students close no tear; no villains.

## 3. Audit against the nine checks

1. **Returning reader oriented through action.** Pass. The packet tells Opus to open on work
   and to reorient by what hands do. Book One's own last page hands over a ready first action
   (see change 1). Drum work "early" gives the right anchors in one scene: Kiva's hands, the
   Step, the dark rule, Mara.
2. **Causal engine, escalation, turn.** Pass, with one sharpening (change 11). The pressure
   ladder is private (what is she for), then professional (the ceiling), then institutional
   (the bill, the captain, the four). The movement-scale turn is that the skill she builds on
   borrowed counts is the one thing the ceiling night needs and she may not use.
3. **Kiva dominant and active.** Pass. Her agency while dark is concrete in the packet: she
   names handoffs, calls counts that are not hers, decides what Hart may read, writes down
   Pike's craft, and sits at the edge of a rule. Opus must keep these as acts, not waiting
   (risk 3 below).
4. **Training, mechanics, Measure, ash, ceiling through attempts.** Pass. The packet's
   teaching list is attempts and consequences (drums bend, the yard fails her, the night
   ends a master's hands, the re-reading measures, the warrant bites). Change 4 repairs the
   one place where the ash bill was stated falsely.
5. **Van set piece planted and consequential.** Pass after change 8. It was planted by the
   dark weeks in principle; it now must also be the first, failed meeting with a Movement Two
   behavior, so that Coombe's drills answer a wound already on the page.
6. **Calendar, injuries, cards, doors, ash, money, knowledge, continuity.** Four exactness
   defects were found and fixed (changes 1, 4, 6, 7). Others recorded below.
7. **47,500 to 52,500 words without padding.** Pass. Counting required developed scenes at
   realistic lengths: drum work (two scenes), the four's yard, the card, Orla and Hart (the
   decision, the telling, the copying), Pike's habits in seven columns, the re-reading and
   relit doors, van night one, van night two with aftermath, the finger test, the contest,
   Kellan at the Rowan table, Perrin's captaincy, the warrant, Saltmere's answer, and three
   or four cutaways comes to roughly 33,000 to 38,000 words. That leaves 12,000 to 15,000 for
   humor, weather, food, Jude and Pru and Anwen beats, Dray and Brask touches, and the
   connective life the packet asks for. It is neither starved nor padded. The risk is the
   other direction: two van nights at 5,000 words each plus a long contest could push past
   52,500. The packet says complete scenes govern, which is the right rule.
8. **Formula usable without chapter contracts.** Pass. The shares, development cadence,
   progression density (80 per 10,000, which I re-derived from 58 and 1.7: front third 79.9,
   rest 47.0), and cutaway budget (13% of the range is 6,175 to 6,825 words, matching the
   packet's 6,200 to 6,800) are movement-level. Change 3 only stops a duplicated cutaway.
9. **Commercial pull.** Pass. Attachment (the four, Pike and the second), rivalry (Dray, the
   contest, Kellan leaving), humor (Perrin's minding, Pru), wonder (a master's invisible
   craft; Hart's hand; the finger test), dread (the ceiling night), a meaningful choice (what
   Hart reads; the rule's edge; Kellan's four), and an end state (date, bill, captain, a
   catcher writing someone else's method) that points at Coombe. I left the final image to
   the author, as the packet does.

## 4. Every packet change and why

All changes are insertions or one-clause corrections to existing bullets. The packet grew by
49 lines and lost 12.

1. **Where we enter: Mara's sheets.** Was "have gone to the coast." Now: tied, waiting on the
   dresser for the coast boat on Monday the 17th. *Why:* Book One chapter 48 (line 295) has
   Mara say "That's for the coast boat on Monday." `STATE_LEDGER.md` says "sent." The
   manuscript governs. It also hands Opus a physical, consequential first action on the
   first free day.
2. **Measure as a bill and a date.** Added the exact interval: first board Monday the 14th
   of the third month, 44 days after Saturday the 30th of the first month; road south due
   by the end of the second month, 30 days on. *Why:* `BOOK_MAP.md` gives Movement Two's
   entry pressure as "five weeks," which is loose (verified: 30 and 44 days; Monday to
   Monday checks). A "date" on the last page has to be true.
3. **Formula, viewpoint.** Orla's natural cutaway is her deciding to tell, not "with the
   card." *Why:* Book One chapter 48 ends on Orla alone reading Hart's card. A second
   reading would be a repeated scene.
4. **Warrant arithmetic.** Replaced "The book that had 'something over' does not" with the
   real figures. *Why:* it was false. 25.53 less 15.0 leaves 10.53; the planned Ember
   crossings and Coombe's floors (1.8 and 4.0) leave 4.73 before any call reserve. The book
   does not run dry here; its surplus is spoken for. Which five heads depends on Kellan.
   Whether the warrant is paid or only posted is left open, with the exactness requirement.
5. **Orla and the dream.** Added: Orla reads the green book weekly and does not know Kiva
   dreams. *Why:* `STATE_LEDGER.md` section 7. The Hart-copy scene can otherwise leak the
   dream to Orla by accident.
6. **Re-reading, two-door figure.** Added that it is one declared Ember-to-still crossing
   on a sanctioned mast, read by Noa, costing that week's go (about 0.30 of her own half
   dram), never a stack; and that the figures show summer and autumn work, not the dark
   weeks. *Why:* a stack read on the Chair's instruments is an incident under her
   conditions, and "dashes with two" has no lawful route to the frame otherwise.
7. **Drum work.** Added: Kiva is not Bench-passed, touches no municipal cell, stays on the
   pavement per Mara's rule. *Why:* chapter 36 ("not at the Bench"), the Chair's conditions,
   and Movement Six continuity section 10 (only Bench-passed students touch municipal
   cells). Her use at a drum is ground and count.
8. **Second van night.** Added that the thing met should be the first, failed meeting with a
   Movement Two behavior (a count-keeping pack, or a folding hem); and that if Pike's hands
   are the cost, the card's "second to Pike, six nights, two per month" stands and the four
   later nights must be decided and recorded. *Why:* criterion 5, and because Pike's hands
   would otherwise silently void the card.
9. **Finger test.** Grounded in Book One: "something's pushing" is their word; chapter 41
   shows the heel and last two fingers reading while the first two said only "pavement." Test
   them alone, against a steady push and a counted one. *Why:* it keeps the finding lawful
   (pressure sense, not magic) and honest about failing.
10. **Human contest.** Not the Halden License learning fight (Movement Two's, with Kellan
    opposite); if sanctioned, the Chair's 2681 conditions must be written. *Why:* prevents
    stealing Movement Two's set piece, and the ledger records that no 2681 conditions exist.
11. **Escalation ladder.** One sentence: private, then professional, then institutional, each
    stage making the next dearer; scene order still the author's. *Why:* the packet's
    obligations are many and unordered, and the ladder protects against a checklist novel
    without becoming a chapter plan.

## 5. Discrepancies found in files outside the packet (not edited)

- `STATE_LEDGER.md` section 5 says Mara "Sent eleven sheets"; chapter 48 says they leave
  Monday. The packet now governs for Movement One; the ledger should say "to be sent."
- `BOOK_MAP.md` Movement One exit says the team is "short of ash." By ledger arithmetic it is
  not yet short: 10.53 remains after a 15.0 warrant, 4.73 after planned training, and the map
  itself says "short in the second month." The packet now frames this correctly.
- `BOOK_MAP.md` Movement Two entry says "five weeks." True intervals are 30 days to the road
  south and 44 to the first board. The Movement Two packet author should correct it.
- Movement Two ends "on the road south" on Monday the 30th of the second month, and Movement
  Three opens Tuesday the 1st, the gate fortnight, at Saltmere. Two hundred miles by a road,
  a ferry, a river and a coast boat in one night is a travel-time question for the Movement
  Two packet.
- The formula's own arithmetic ("scenes near 950 words" against "8.7 breaks per 10,000") does
  not close (10,000 divided by 950 is about 10.5). `FORMULA.md` says to report this, not
  rewrite it. The packet's text is unchanged.
- The packet's "a fighter stands for one four only, declared at entry" is the packet's rule
  and not in Book One; rule nineteen (Kellan's Regional declaration) is the only precedent.

## 6. Unresolved risks Opus must manage

1. **The dream and Hart's copy.** The green book is read weekly by Orla, who does not know.
   Whatever Kiva withholds must be a line she chooses on the page (Annick's phrase, the 2604
   keeper's line, a private note), and nothing may let Orla infer it. Hart's replies, if any,
   stay in his four-line register, with no meeting and no forgiveness.
2. **Pike's hands versus the card.** Decide and record what happens to the February and March
   nights. Pike's recovery of about six weeks is consistent with the Stroud figure in chapter
   43, but it lands inside the card's term.
3. **Kiva's agency.** Keep her acts as acts: asking Brask, Wace or Dray to hold counts,
   naming parts that are not hers, writing the columns, the edge-of-rule choice. A catcher
   who watches and is told things would fail the dominance check.
4. **The rule-edge choice must cost something.** If she speaks before the bag is sealed, the
   card and Rhea's trust take a mark. If she holds, a master pays for her silence. Either is
   fine; a free third option is not.
5. **Calendar crib.** Eleventh month Mondays: 1, 8, 15, 22, 29. Twelfth month Wednesdays: 1,
   8, 15, 22, 29. First month Fridays: 1, 8, 15, 22, 29; Saturdays: 2, 9, 16, 23, 30. The
   1st of the first month is a Friday and the movement ends Saturday the 30th. Aske's "eleven
   weeks and four days" from Monday the 10th to the new year is correct (81 days).
6. **The seven drums are an early engine.** The ledger says one a day, so they finish within
   about a week of the 17th unless the page gives a reason (the wardens' stock is finite and
   of unknown tune; a pinch that is not quite plain is a fair way for one drum to refuse).
7. **Money and ash must close to the hundredth** at the exit: every dram spent, lent, earned
   or refused entered; the red bags untouched; no free practice fuel; the 0.8 reserve opened
   only for a shown medical need.
8. **Progression density at 80 per 10,000** can turn the page into a stat block. Carry it by
   attempts, corrections and arguments, not by quoted figures.
9. **Rhythm.** Book One's clipped dialogue is a standing pull. Keep long, well-ordered
   sentences in narration (observation, spatial setup, reasoning), and do not chase the
   secondary figures.
10. **Rival thinness.** Dray is in a chair and Coombe is announced only. A letter, a note on
    the re-reading, or a glimpse from a window would keep rivalry alive without breaking the
    boundary. Optional.
11. **Sanctioned versus practice.** If the contest is a sanctioned event, write the Chair's
    2681 conditions. A yard contest avoids this.
12. **Name checks** for any new minor name against `universe/NAME_REGISTRY.md` and the
    manuscripts, as the packet requires.

## 7. Verdict

The packet was already strong. The eleven changes are four exactness repairs (sheets, warrant
arithmetic, two-door reading, Bench status), three consequence-linking additions (van night,
Pike and the card, contest collision), one knowledge-boundary guard, one test-design
grounding, one cutaway fix, and one escalation sentence. None adds a chapter card or removes
Opus's room.

READY FOR OPUS: YES

Brask pulls the red handle; Perrin carries Mr. Garland out in his chair with his bird. |
| 11:30 | The blue reaches the stone sill of the black gate and stops there; inside the gate the main dives to eight feet. **The twelfth knot stands in the gateway.** Vane to the wardens: the gate is shut to them; "whatever's left of the top comes down, by the curb, behind the porch, with its boxes dark." Ansty draws the pin and puts the School's core down. |
| about 11:34 | **An arm, three-and-twenty paces long,** goes from the twelfth knot along the foot of the bottom wall, through the gap, round the lane side of the leaning post, and down beside the half-dragged lid into the sluice shaft. Perrin lifts Ned out by one arm first. The ten standing knots lean back together and ease: it is pumping the main. |
| 11:42 | Mara's slip: nineteen hands of twenty-four (a dozen of the Works' night shift were on the floor already); pumps stopped two minutes on trial; the water "moved less at twenty past eleven, when I came in and chalked it"; **"The water is still moving… I cannot fill over this. Tell me when it is out."** |
| to about 11:55 | Nobody looks at Kiva. She says what it is, who has every piece that is not hers, and what she does if it is refused. Selka Dray arrives in an infirmary chair. Aske says the price. Rhea writes *Before.* Orla signs for the hand. Vane reads the direction. Anwen is fetched from the wall and reads the *why.* "I'm frightened." "Then yes." |
| | Four minutes to set. The step first: **four paces east is not clean (the main is under it). Kiva turns the mark north,** across the lane, and has it said back. She sees that the hem will then hang between the mark and the post, so whoever has the corner cannot be at the bar: Brask and Perrin to the bar, Wace and Moss to the mark. |
| about 11:57 | **"Hand, to Ember, to still. Rowan, changing on three, and on five."** On Dray's *in* the ward goes under the arm; on the haul it throws her a quarter round, north; fold; gap; bound; gap; **"Planted! Six!"** Ned shouts it to the lodge. The Works stops its pumps and quenches all twenty-four cells. |
| | At about nine she leans the corner to Wace's plank, with Moss at its other end, and shuts. Yarrow of Halden walks up and makes a third. She calls fives against Dray's fours for the best part of three hundred counts. |
| | Perrin and Brask (cutaway): eight turns; the ninth sticks on a hand of silt; then goes. **"Down!"** |
| | Mara (cutaway): the sump water rocks less, then lies flat; she sends *Still.* |
| about 12:02 | **"Back!"** on Dray's *in.* Three still-doors give the corner to the ground. **Ten blades cut ten knots** (Rhea Sorn the twelfth, in the gateway; Kellan the eleventh; Jude the tenth; Yeo the fourth). The hem lies down from the mark to the gate and down the hill. **Every window in the School and six hundred on the hill show a point of light once.** |
| | With nothing to hold it the seam goes on giving for a little: three husks (Jude, Gower, Twenty-Eight's reserve blade), then a string of four that stands on end and goes to the gate. Anwen calls the step in the middle of ten wards; Rhea Sorn stands behind its legs to cut it, with Nairn beside her. The closing crew works up the hill from the foot. |
| about 12:23 | The third pump starts. **The pumps stood twenty-six minutes.** |
| 12:25 | First slip: three pumps on three counts; the bank is out. |
| about 12:35 | Second slip, in Kellan's mother's words (off the wire): Mara's reading hand is starved to the wrist. |
| 12:40 | **The sealed medical reserve is opened by four** on the Low Lane's quench box. |
| **12:50** | **"Closed!"** (Bettan). |
| about 1:00 | The pump house: Aske eases Mara's hand; the keeper's book of 2604; Adlam's line. |

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan.** Third Division, Crooked Line third team. **Seated; no longer provisional;
"Catcher."** Sponsor's line and every certificate condition unchanged; "not thereby
classified" (the Chair, the 14th).
- **On the Home Line's roll as catcher** from the 13th: not a seat, not a wage beyond a
  line-hand's day rate when called; called by a Line foreman, with her sponsor's leave while
  she has one, "to set a mark, to read ground, to call a count that is hers and name the ones
  that are not."
- **Three doors.** One more, in the field, on the 5th, under a Home Line direction and Rhea
  Sorn's hand, **offered and not asked.** Still uncertified, unclassified and "not to be asked
  of her." Three clean in all (a yard; the Pit; the Old Yard). Never twice inside a fortnight.
- **Injuries.** The left arm went candle-white to a hand's breadth above the elbow. Elbow back
  Friday the 7th toward evening; wrist Saturday night; palm, thumb and last two fingers Sunday
  morning. **Aske, Monday the 10th: "as before… and not a hair less."** The first two left
  fingers are unchanged: they bend, grip and report pressure and nothing else. **Nothing lit
  until the new year** ("not a step, not a ward for a tap"). The knee is still strapped and
  complained once, running.
- **The disk.** Cold on the 5th before, during and after; cold on the oak table in front of
  twelve people on the 13th. Fourth column entry of the 10th in section 4.
- **The dream.** No recurrence. No fever is mentioned on any night of this movement.
- What she now knows: a count that she takes through two hands is wrong by half a count; a
  planted corner can be leaned to another still-door as a plank; a ward curved the other way
  turns a throw a quarter round; the nearest road for a throw may be the one with the drain
  under it; the notch on her pen and the lamps' T-0 are two things.
- **Handoffs named** (the list of the 30th, as it stands at exit): the cord, all three turns
  (Pru); the slack of a hem on her left side (she takes a right-hand corner or is called to);
  tin lids with a lip; the pin of a seal and whether wax has gone off (Anwen has the wax); a
  button and a cuff; **the count of a thing that keeps time (somebody else's, named before).**
- **The pattern.** Answers withheld: at the Halden yard ("Sorn's right that it isn't hers to
  have offered"); at the leaning post on the 1st ("Tell me… Not mend it"); to the junior on
  the 16th. One taken and paid for: she knelt and called the slack herself on the 2nd, half a
  count late, and nipped Pru's fingers; she said so to the yard.
- **Offers.** Rhea Sorn's card (section 4), signed on the back by her four on the 15th.
- Her book has never had a page torn out. It has a spring, a summer and an autumn in it.

**Mara Rowan.** Reader of the trunk for Rusk; found that the drums fall in in the order the
Ferren main reaches them; put it under seal and was right. **Given the hill, the bank and the
stopping of it on the 3rd.** Found the sluice. Built the trough. Left the hill on the night
("I can only manage it from two miles off"). Took the Ferren bank out of step with
twenty-four hands, each cell on its own count, standing between each and the next with her
left palm; gave the odd cells to Rusk at the eighteenth. **Her left, reading hand was starved
to the wrist and was eased inside the hour with 1.2 drams of Team Seven's sealed ward-trace.**
A week before she reads a drum; a month before she reads a bank. Has written eleven sheets in
seven columns, *How the Ferren bank was taken out of step, with what was got wrong first,* for
the coast boat. Her sealed page is still sealed.

**Orla Dane.** Refused Vane's saw without a reason and said so. Climbed the Graywater stair to
tell Rhea Sorn that neither of them signs for the girl. Signed for the hand on the 5th: "I've
seen it clean twice. I've seen it missed three times… And I don't know that it will work."
Moved her crate a pace north. **Jessamy Hart has answered** (section 4). She did not write in
the proof; she gave it to Kiva.

**Anwen Pryce.** Has the wax. Had the sealed reserve out of the press on the 5th. Refused it as
salt on the 4th and proposed it as medicine on the 6th. Stood the uphill end of a walking
